I curate and only recommend products and services I would use or book myself.*\n\nTwo to three times a week, I took a break from my photo editing, to go out to hunting for one thing – a good lunch menu find. Not only do I get in my fresh air and steps, I’m exploring new corners of the cities and finding restaurants with a particular interesting or cheap lunch menu. It’s one of my favorite things about Poland. Even some of the most upscale restaurants will offer some kind of discounted set menu.\n\nAs much as I love a good baked pierogi stuffed with caramelized onions and melted cheese or a warm bigos (“Hunter’s Stew”) with slow cooked beef, part of what makes the Polish scene special is a desire to bring in international cuisine and a willingness to keep it authentic. So below are some of my favorite restaurants and lunch menus in Krakow. It’s a mix of both local and international food. I’ve also linked to their Google Maps location, so you can save it for later.\n\nI’ll quote the prices in PLN, but for reference I’m using an exchange rate of 1 USD = 3.65 PLN as of August 2025.\n\n## Best Lunch Menus and Cheap Eats in Krakow\n\nHere’s what I have to say about Krakow. There are so many good places to choose from in this youthful city, but if you’re visiting for the first time, here are my favorite go to spots and recommendations.\n\n### Cafe Manggha\n\nIt may seem crazy that the first place I recommend for everyone visiting Krakow is [a little Japanese cafe](https://maps.app.goo.gl/L8pPUxksmttfxRgx5) in a museum, but hear me out.\n\n[Cafe Manggha](https://manggha.pl/cafe-manggha) is great for breakfast or lunch or both. The [Manggha Center](https://manggha.pl/en) is a Japanese Cultural Center that hosts impressive exhibitions of art from Japan, usually in collaboration with the Japan Foundation. This collaborative exchange of culture and knowledge has paved way for a cafe that brings the authentic taste of Japan to Krakow.\n\nThe simplicity and minimalism of this place make it one of my favorite Japanese dining experience in Poland.\n\n**Breakfast** is done in the Teishoku style where you choose from a selection of bento boxes that includes all the essentials of a Japanese set breakfast: a miso soup, rice, a protein dish, a pickled dish, a veggie dish, an egg dish, and a little dessert. The meal comes with a coffee or a herbal tea for between 34 and 37 PLN.\n\n**Lunch** offers similar sets for between 42 and 46 PLN. Breakfast is available from 9 to 1 pm and then lunch is from 1 to 5:30 pm. One day, I actually went for breakfast and then returned at 5 pm and had their lunch just because I was missing Japanese food so much. There is even a dedicated tea master to prepare the different teas in the Japanese style.\n\n### Pod Roza\n\n[Pod Roza](https://maps.app.goo.gl/tDf4UN7bc6yetyvz6) is part of the beautiful Pod Roza hotel and offers a unique lunch tasting menu for 89 PLN on weekdays from 12 to 4 pm.\n\nThe [tasting menu](https://hotel.com.pl/en/pod-roza-restaurant) starts with a tray of five small dishes, each of which could easily be its own course on a different restaurant’s tasting menu. My appetizer tray was described as this:  bread/butter, a mushroom/kefir, arancini/pumpkin, wild broccoli/curry and tuna/apple. It was superb and just the beginning.\n\nThe main dish of the day was lamb and vegetable skewers served over a raita yogurt sauce. Dessert was a chocolate cake of sort with a layer of tasty elderflower “film” draped on top. Their menu changes regularly.\n\nThis was probably the best value lunch menu I had in Krakow and cannot speak any higher of their service and cuisine.\n\n### Copernicus\n\n[Copernicus](https://maps.app.goo.gl/xUVEGTF7UCXqZoUw7) is a centrally located [upscale restaurant](https://www.relaischateaux.com/us/poland/restaurant/hotel-copernicus-malopolska-krakow) just steps from the old town. The chef takes old Polish recipes and reinterprets them in a modern way. Their normal five and seven-course tasting menus cost 390 PLN and 440 PLN, respectively, but they also offer a three-course lunch menu for 119 PLN.\n\nAnd yet somehow you get so much more than the price gap suggests. The meal starts off with an amuse bouche, that would qualify as a starter in other restaurants, followed by a large selection of homemade breads accompanied by butter, vegan lard, a fruity paste, and a nut covered cheese roll. Again, the presentation alone was worthy of it being considered a standalone course.\n\nOn the day I visited, my starter was gnocchi with pumpkin and chorizo, followed by salmon with corn and baked carrot, and a white chocolate cream with passion fruit. The dessert looked more like a modernist piece of art than a pastry. But we weren’t done yet. The meal concluded with a plate of five different cookies and pastries. It was a shame it took me so long to get to this restaurant.\n\nIt’s available Monday – Friday from 12 pm to 3 pm.\n\nThis was on par with Pod Roza for being one of my the best lunches in Krakow, notwithstanding the incredible value.\n\n### Karakter\n\n[Karakter](https://maps.app.goo.gl/STjHkNrZ3j3GZAs27) has a chill atmosphere and is one of those restaurants where everything on the menu jumps out at you. For starters, they have less common tartare dishes made with horse and ostrich. They also have a veal brain pate and an orzo pasta with beef marrow. If you are feeling a little adventurous, this is the place I recommend.\n\nFor main, they had dishes like a sweetbread made with horse thymus, slow roasted duck leg in ponzu, and a beef burger with smoked mangalica lard. They also have a tapas menu and a mussel bar with 6 different sauces.\n\nThe a la carte menus is why I came, but there’s also a three-course lunch menu on weekdays for 55 PLN and includes a starter, main, and dessert. The mussels bar lunch menu is 59 PLN. You can find the latest menus on their Facebook [page](https://www.facebook.com/karakter.restauracja/).\n\nI had the tempura fried cheddar cheese in a cherry and beetroot cream with white chocolate for the starter, the hanger steak with a passion fruit butter and mango/baked shallots for the main and a slightly strange tomato ice cream with spinach, honey, and eggnog.\n\n### Zazie Bistro\n\nFor a little escape to France, [Zazie Bistro](https://maps.app.goo.gl/xui4cbFF5pYo2iUE8) is the place to go. The ute bistro cafe has a classically [French menu](http://www.zaziebistro.pl/menu-eng.html). I came here twice, once for their lunch menu, which comes with a choice of starter, a main and dessert for 41 PLN. It was great value for a starter of goat cheese and onion jam baked in filo dough, mussels in white wine and cream, and a mini creme brûlée.\n\nOn my return visit, I went the la carte route and ordered the Bourguinon beef cheeks, and the asparagus with wild garlic and straciatella cheese. Amazing. There are so still so many dishes I want to try at Zazie.\n\nPRO TIP: If you are looking for a great place to stay in Krakow, I highly recommend the [Apartamenty Kossak Residence](https://www.booking.com/hotel/pl/apartamenty-kossak-residence.en.html?aid=2073577&no_rooms=1&group_adults=2). the beautifully designed studio was everything i needed for a week. and its location by the castle and the old town was perfect to explore the restaurants close by.\n\n### Smakolyki\n\nFor good old classic Polish cuisines that doesn’t try to be more or less, head to [Smakolyki](https://maps.app.goo.gl/1CoV7bbDJLGVDWDh9) Smakolyki. Their mains range from 26 and 58 PLN, but they also have a daily soup + main + drink for 36 PLN. The restaurant is set in a large, well-lit space that’s simple yet pleasant. You can find all the [hearty Polish favorites](http://www.smakolyki.eu) here like bigos, tripe, chicken liver with fried onions, and stuffed beef rolls with dumplings. They also offer healthier salad options.\n\n### Ranny Ptaszek\n\nI’m not vegetarian, but I don’t mind going to a vegetarian only restaurant like [Ranny Ptaszek](https://maps.app.goo.gl/wXbWpWTGBkPbibn18) when it comes to brunch. This cozy and inviting spot is great for brunch with friends. Their menu is simple, but every dish is so photogenic.\n\nI ordered the shakshuka and shared one of the bowls with my girlfriend.\n\n### Hevre\n\n[Hevre](https://maps.app.goo.gl/nXRn74K2sngcyN2k7) might have one of the most impressive and dramatic settings in Krakow. The restaurant is built in a former synagogue and gives old world, you’ve just stepped back in time vibes. The [menu here](https://www.facebook.com/hevrekazimierz/menu/) is straightforward European, but well-executed, and the ambiance is what makes this place special. They also hosts events here.\n\n### Oranzeria\n\n[Cafe Oranzeria](https://maps.app.goo.gl/L8Vs22t56eLHzqUs7) is rooftop restaurant with one of the best dining views in Krakow, overlooking the castle and the river. Their classical European [menu](https://cafeoranzeria.pl/uploads/pdf/Menu%20Restauracja%20Cafe%20Oranżeria%20lato_jesień%202024.pdf) is can seem generic, but the execution is excellent. Make a reservation and ask for a seat by the window or outside. I visited on a weekend for drinks and snacks, so I don’t recall if they have a lunch menu, but it’s worth checking out.\n\n### Milkbar Tomasza\n\nIt would be blasphemous to leave a milk bar off a list of lunch recommendations for Krakow. Bar mleczny or milk bars, are like the beating heart of Polish dining. They are low-cost cafeteria style restaurants that are living relics from the Communist era. There are many scattered around the center of Krakow. [Tomasza](https://maps.app.goo.gl/QiDpFcroCvFw18iE9) is an authentic milk bar serving all the [typical local dishes](https://www.facebook.com/photo/?fbid=840255437904555&set=p.840255437904555), but is also tourist-friendly when it comes to language.\n\nLunch starts at 12 pm with dishes around 33 PLN. I try a different milk bar every week.I loved their breaded camembert and the dumplings with pickled cabbage and wild mushrooms.\n\nIf you come for breakfast, treat yourself to the pancakes with cottage cheese or the krokiety (croquettes).\n\n### Kolanko No 6\n\nI like [Kolanko No 6](https://maps.app.goo.gl/ZwEFCHthrbDTZXeXA) as much for their food as their quirky interior decorations. It gives travelers vibe and is a fun place to sit out back to soak in the sun. They do a breakfast buffet here for 42 PLN from 8 am to 12 pm everyday and offer a [lunch menu](http://kolanko.net/menu) for 35 PLN with a different meat or vegetarian main every day, along with a soup and fruit drink.\n\nThe food is an eclectic mix of dishes from around the world, like Indonesian chicken satay, Tunisian Shatshuka, Indian butter chicken, and Greek Moussaka. I joke that this is the best place for a group of people indecisive eaters.\n\n### Soup Culture\n\nWhen you really want soup, but want something more than just a borscht, there’s [Soup Culture](https://maps.app.goo.gl/tqgDTNcgFjxTFiHj8). They have so many options here that it can be overwhelming. The best part? The container is an edible bread cone-shaped cup you can eat on the go, along with your favorite toppings.\n\nHappy eating! If you’re traveling in Poland, check out my food guides for all the other cities.\n\nLooking for more travel inspirations? Follow me [@hellokien](https://instagram.com/hellokien) or scroll through some of the 450+ experiences on my [bucket list](#bucketlist).       "title": "Poland Food Guide: Must-Try Dishes, Prices, and Tips",
      "content": "Think pierogi, żurek, bigos, kiełbasa, and Polish doughnuts as your core Poland must try food, then branch out into regional specialities like oscypek cheese in the Tatra mountains and herring in coastal cities. A quick-service meal in Warsaw or Kraków usually costs 25–45 PLN ($6–11) in 2026, while a sit-down dinner at a mid-range restaurant runs 60–120 PLN ($15–30) per person including a drink.\n\nStreet food is excellent value: zapiekanka (Polish baguette pizza) from a stall might cost 12–20 PLN ($3–5), and a serving of pierogi from a bar mleczny (milk bar) can be under 25 PLN ($6). Vegetarian options are easy to find in major cities; vegan and halal are available but may take a bit more research. [...] Traditional Polish cuisine leans heavily on meat, dairy, and eggs, but the modern food scene is diverse. In Warsaw and Kraków, you’ll find purely vegan restaurants, vegetarian milk bars, and plant-based burger joints, plus many cafes offering oat or soy milk. A vegan main in 2026 typically costs 35–55 PLN ($9–13), similar to regular mains.\n\nFor vegetarians, pierogi ruskie (potato and cheese), cheese and mushroom zapiekanka, meat-free soups, and salads are easy staples. Many menus mark dishes with V or VEGAN, though not everywhere, so check ingredients or ask staff. Gluten-free travellers should be cautious with breaded meats and dumplings; some places now offer gluten-free pierogi and pizza, especially in health-focused cafes. [...] Start with pierogi, Poland’s iconic dumplings, usually filled with potato and cheese (ruskie), meat, or sauerkraut and mushrooms. In 2026, a serving in a casual restaurant in Kraków or Warsaw costs around 25–40 PLN ($6–10), with upscale venues charging 45–60 PLN ($11–15) for gourmet versions. Classic soups like żurek (sour rye soup with sausage and egg) or barszcz czerwony (beetroot soup, sometimes served with small dumplings) range from 18–30 PLN ($4.50–7.50).",

      "raw_content": "Food & Dining8 min read\n\n# Poland Food Guide: Must-Try Dishes, Prices, and Tips\n\nBest local dishes, street food, restaurant prices, dietary options, and food safety tips for Poland.\n\nBy Hello Travel Team\n\nThis article contains affiliate links. If you book through them, Hello may earn a commission at no additional cost to you. Our editorial recommendations are independent.[Learn more](/affiliate-disclosure).\n\n![Poland Food Guide: Must-Try Dishes, Prices, and Tips](https://cdn.homejourney.sg/hello-travel/poland-2185ec4c.png)\n\n## TL;DR: What to Eat in Poland and How Much It Costs\n\nPoland’s food scene is all about hearty comfort dishes, generous portions, and very fair prices, with most travellers spending around $20–35 per day on food in 2026 if they mix street food and casual restaurants. You’ll eat well on a budget, especially if you know what to try and where.\n\nThink **pierogi, żurek, bigos, kiełbasa, and Polish doughnuts** as your core Poland must try food, then branch out into regional specialities like oscypek cheese in the Tatra mountains and herring in coastal cities. A quick-service meal in Warsaw or Kraków usually costs 25–45 PLN ($6–11) in 2026, while a sit-down dinner at a mid-range restaurant runs 60–120 PLN ($15–30) per person including a drink.\n\nStreet food is excellent value: zapiekanka (Polish baguette pizza) from a stall might cost 12–20 PLN ($3–5), and a serving of pierogi from a bar mleczny (milk bar) can be under 25 PLN ($6). Vegetarian options are easy to find in major cities; vegan and halal are available but may take a bit more research.\n\nTo keep track of food expenses and split shared meals with friends, the **Hello app** is handy: AI receipt scanning in any language and currency, automatic exchange rates, and multi-currency budgeting mean you can see exactly how much your Poland food adventure is costing in real time.\n\n## Poland Must-Try Food: Classic Dishes You Should Start With\n\nThe best Poland must try food focuses on simple ingredients turned into comforting dishes, with pierogi, żurek, bigos, and kotlet schabowy forming the core of most traditional menus across the country. If you eat these four, you’ve had the essential Polish food experience almost anywhere you go.\n\nStart with **pierogi**, Poland’s iconic dumplings, usually filled with potato and cheese (ruskie), meat, or sauerkraut and mushrooms. In 2026, a serving in a casual restaurant in Kraków or Warsaw costs around 25–40 PLN ($6–10), with upscale venues charging 45–60 PLN ($11–15) for gourmet versions. Classic soups like **żurek** (sour rye soup with sausage and egg) or **barszcz czerwony** (beetroot soup, sometimes served with small dumplings) range from 18–30 PLN ($4.50–7.50).\n\nFor meat lovers, **bigos** (hunter’s stew of cabbage, sauerkraut, and mixed meats) and **kotlet schabowy** (breaded pork cutlet, similar to schnitzel) are staples of Polish home cooking and restaurant menus; expect to pay 35–55 PLN ($9–13) for a hearty portion in 2026. In coastal cities like Gdańsk, fresh **herring** and fried fish are popular, while in Zakopane and the Tatra region, smoked **oscypek** cheese served grilled with cranberry jam is a must-try street snack.\n\nDessert-wise, look for **sernik** (Polish cheesecake), **szarlotka** (apple cake) and **pączki** (filled doughnuts), often 8–15 PLN ($2–4) each in bakeries. Together, these dishes form a reliable starter checklist for any Poland food guide.\n\n## Street Food vs Restaurants in Poland: Prices, Portions, and Where to Eat\n\nPoland street food offers some of the best value in Europe, with filling snacks from stalls and milk bars often costing under $6, while mid-range restaurant meals average $15–30 per person in 2026, including a drink and generous portions. Mixing both is the easiest way to stretch your food budget.\n\nYou’ll find classic Poland street food like **zapiekanka** (half-baguette topped with mushrooms, cheese, and sauces), grilled kiełbasa, and oscypek at popular spots such as Plac Nowy in Kraków and street markets around Warsaw’s old town. Typical prices in 2026 are:\n\n| Type of meal | Typical price (PLN) | Approx. price (USD) |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Street snack (zapiekanka, oscypek) | 12–20 | $3–5 |\n| Fast-food combo or kebab | 18–30 | $4.50–7.50 |\n| Milk bar lunch (soup + main) | 25–40 | $6–10 |\n| Casual sit-down dinner | 60–120 | $15–30 |\n| Upscale restaurant tasting menu | 200–350 | $50–85 |\n\n**Bar mleczny (milk bars)** are uniquely Polish and perfect for budget travellers: simple cafeterias serving home-style dishes, often subsidized historically, with daily specials written on boards. It’s common to get soup, pierogi, and a drink for under 35 PLN ($8) in big cities.\n\nRestaurant culture is relaxed, and casual diners aren’t typically rushed off tables. Menus increasingly include English translations in tourist areas, though smaller local spots may only have Polish. Here’s where **Hello’s AI receipt scanning** helps: snap a photo of any Polish receipt and the app automatically reads the amount, converts it into your home currency, and categorizes the meal for budget tracking.\n\n## Dietary Needs in Poland: Vegetarian, Vegan, Halal, and Food Allergies\n\nPoland is increasingly friendly to vegetarian and vegan travellers, especially in big cities like Warsaw, Kraków, Wrocław, and Gdańsk, while halal options exist but may require more planning; most restaurants handle common food allergies if you ask clearly and in advance.\n\nTraditional Polish cuisine leans heavily on meat, dairy, and eggs, but the modern food scene is diverse. In Warsaw and Kraków, you’ll find **purely vegan restaurants, vegetarian milk bars, and plant-based burger joints**, plus many cafes offering oat or soy milk. A vegan main in 2026 typically costs 35–55 PLN ($9–13), similar to regular mains.\n\nFor vegetarians, pierogi ruskie (potato and cheese), cheese and mushroom zapiekanka, meat-free soups, and salads are easy staples. Many menus mark dishes with **V** or **VEGAN**, though not everywhere, so check ingredients or ask staff. Gluten-free travellers should be cautious with breaded meats and dumplings; some places now offer gluten-free pierogi and pizza, especially in health-focused cafes.\n\nHalal dining is centered mainly in larger cities, often via Middle Eastern, Turkish, or Indian restaurants, plus some kebab shops using halal meat. Searching terms like “halal restaurant Warsaw” before you go, then saving a shortlist, is wise. Muslim travellers can also rely on seafood and vegetarian dishes in non-halal venues.\n\nIf you’re tracking different dietary expenses among friends, **Hello’s expense splitting** lets you tag meals, split costs in multiple currencies, and automatically apply exchange rates so everyone pays fairly—useful when one friend orders a full meat feast and another sticks to vegan options.\n\n## Food Safety, Hygiene, and Tipping Etiquette in Poland\n\nFood safety standards in Poland are generally high, tap water in most cities is safe to drink, and restaurant hygiene is comparable to Western Europe, so common sense—rather than worry—is your best guide when deciding where to eat and drink.\n\nPoland is part of the European Union, so restaurants and food producers follow EU-level safety regulations, and inspections are routine. As of 2025, Poland hosted over 30 million international visitors annually according to national tourism statistics, and there’s strong incentive to maintain hygiene standards in major tourist hubs. Street food is usually safe if cooked fresh and served hot; avoid items that look like they’ve been sitting out too long or aren’t kept warm.\n\nTap water in cities like Warsaw, Kraków, and Wrocław is generally considered safe, but many locals still prefer bottled water, so you may do the same if you’re concerned. In restaurants, it’s common to order still or sparkling water rather than getting free tap water.\n\nTipping culture is moderate: at sit-down restaurants, **10% is typical** if service was good, and rounding up the bill at cafes or for small snacks is appreciated but not mandatory. Always check if a service charge is included—this is less common than in some Western countries but may appear in touristy areas.\n\nTo stay organized, use **Hello’s budget tracking** and voice expense entry to quickly log restaurant bills and tips after paying. Saying something like “Dinner in Warsaw, 90 PLN including tip” creates an expense entry that the app converts into your home currency and categorizes automatically.\n\n## Staying Connected and Ordering Food in Poland with Apps and Hello eSIM\n\nReliable data makes Poland’s food scene much easier to navigate, from checking reviews to ordering delivery, and an **eSIM from Hello** lets you arrive already connected so you can immediately find pierogi spots, milk bars, and the best bakeries near your accommodation.\n\nIn cities, food delivery apps are widely used, especially for those who want to sample local restaurants from their hotel or apartment. With fast mobile data and Wi‑Fi, you can browse menus, see photos, compare prices, and read reviews in English. Ordering online is particularly helpful if you have dietary restrictions and want to double-check ingredients without language pressure at the counter.\n\nUsing a **Hello eSIM for Poland** gives you instant data on arrival, with plans starting from 5GB and activation done before you even land. You can buy and activate via the Hello app or website, then step off the plane in Warsaw or Kraków ready to open maps, translation apps, or food delivery platforms without hunting for a SIM kiosk. For other stops in Central Europe, similar coverage is available on routes like [Hello eSIM for Japan](/esim/japan) when you continue your trip abroad.\n\nAs you explore, it’s smart to mark favourite restaurants on your map and take photos of receipts. Hello’s **AI receipt scanning** in any language (including Polish) reads the total, applies live exchange rates, and logs it so you can see how much you’ve spent on street food vs restaurants over the trip, which is great for budgeting future travels.\n\n## Common Questions: What to Eat in Poland, Budget, and Local Customs\n\nMost travellers can eat very well in Poland on $20–35 per day in 2026 by combining street food, milk bars, and casual restaurants, focusing on pierogi, soups, and local snacks while following simple hygiene and tipping customs similar to other European countries.\n\n**Q: What are the must-try Polish dishes for first-time visitors?**  \n A: Start with pierogi, żurek, bigos, kotlet schabowy, and oscypek if you visit the mountains. Add sernik or pączki for dessert, and you’ll have tasted the core of traditional Polish cuisine.\n\n**Q: How much should I budget for food per day in Poland?**  \n A: If you’re mixing Poland street food and sit-down meals, expect roughly:\n\n* Budget travellers: 60–90 PLN ($15–22) per day in 2026, mainly milk bars and snacks.\n* Mid-range: 80–140 PLN ($20–35) per day, with one restaurant meal plus coffee and dessert.\n* Higher-end: 160+ PLN ($40+) per day with wine, cocktails, or fine dining.\n\n**Q: Is it easy to eat vegetarian or vegan in Poland?**  \n A: Yes in bigger cities; you’ll find dedicated vegan spots and plenty of meat-free options. In smaller towns, choices are more limited but pierogi ruskie, salads, and soups are usually safe bets.\n\n**Q: Do I need to tip at restaurants?**  \n A: Tipping 10% is standard if service is good. At cafes or for small snacks, rounding up a few złoty is appreciated.\n\n**Q: How can I track my food spending and split restaurant bills with friends?**  \n A: Use the Hello app: you can scan receipts in Polish, import bank statements, and split expenses in multiple currencies, with automatic exchange rates, which is especially useful on group trips across [Poland](/poland) and neighbouring countries.\n\n## Explore These Destinations\n\n[Poland](/poland)\n\n## Stay Connected\n\n### 🇵🇱eSIM Plans for Poland\n\n[5 GB\n\n30days ·       "raw_content": "[![Bona Restaurant](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/clients/1692012715_bona-logo-07.png)](/) \n\n[Book now](#section-20099)\n\n* [![pl](https://cdn.bookero.pl/assets/images/pages/flags/pl.svg) pl](/pl)\n* [![en](https://cdn.bookero.pl/assets/images/pages/flags/en.svg) en](/en)\n\n\n\n* ![New Italian cuisine in the heart of Krakow](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/banners/1692012321_366071172-122125396334001374-7378214449805436204-n.jpg)\n\n  # New Italian cuisine in the heart of Krakow\n\n## Book a table\n\n![CUISINE](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1696587992_16938330278981-projektbeznazwy10.jpg)\n\n## CUISINE\n\n## Restaurant Bona – an Italian restaurant in the heart of Kraków\n\nBona offers Italian cuisine in an elegant, signature interpretation. Tradition meets modern flair and the Chef’s artistic touch. Here, you will taste authentic Italian products in surprising combinations. You will discover culinary techniques rarely seen in everyday dining. The diverse menu features exquisite meats, fish, seafood, cheeses, and desserts beloved by Italians. The experience is completed by the restaurant’s atmosphere – at Bona, guests are surrounded by art at every turn. This Italian restaurant in the center of Kraków is the perfect place for those who appreciate flawlessly crafted experiences.\n\n![CUISINE](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1696588148_16938332524865-projektbeznazwy12.jpg)\n\n## CUISINE\n\n## Italian cuisine in Kraków\n\nExplore the full menu of Bona Restaurant and, on your next visit to the center of Kraków, rediscover Italian cuisine. Taste something you’ve never tried before… or, on the contrary, indulge in a creative twist on a dish you already know well. Passion, precision, and respect for every ingredient are the hallmarks of our culinary masters.\n\n[CHECK MENU](https://profitroom-uploads.fra1.digitaloceanspaces.com/thecrownhotelhandwrittencollection/17038507572754_MENU%20ALA%20CARTE.pdf)\n\n![BREAKFAST](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1696596462_16940741347314-pg230824058optimized.jpg)\n\n## BREAKFAST\n\n## Elegant Breakfast in Kraków\n\nWe invite you to enjoy an exceptional breakfast crafted from ingredients sourced from local suppliers, served with a touch of Italian tradition. To begin, we present an étagère – a varied selection of sweet and savory bites. Guests are free to compose their own sets, tailored to their taste. Naturally, fresh bread is never missing, and each table is provided with a menu of warm dishes prepared by our breakfast chef. Start your day with a perfect breakfast in a calm and refined atmosphere.\n\n[CHECK MENU](https://profitroom-uploads.fra1.digitaloceanspaces.com/thecrownhotelhandwrittencollection/1695301390958_KRK_TC_SNIADANIE_A5_%20PION_Czarna%20wersja.pdf)\n\n![](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1723109000_16940741279785-pg230824066optimized.jpg)\n\n### Breakfast Hours\n\nOur breakfasts are available for you every day.\n\nDaily 06:30 – 10:30\n\nPRICE: 90 PLN per person\n\nContact: **fb.manager@crownkrakow.pl**\n\n[CHECK MENU](https://www.crownhotel.pl/eventy/late-breakfast)\n\n* ![Italian Style Lunch](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/banners/1723110808_17060955609189-grafikidostroninewsletterw20240124t122403068.jpg)\n\n  # Italian Style Lunch\n\n## Italian Lunches – Discover New Flavors Every Week!\n\nItalian inspirations never come to an end. We strive to rediscover the tastes of Italy and deliver unforgettable, one-of-a-kind experiences to our guests. Join us in exploring the richness of Italian cuisine through our ever-changing lunch menu. Every week, the chef at Bona Restaurant creates new, original compositions of dishes and desserts.\n\nCurrent Lunch Menu – **[Click Here](https://www.crownhotel.pl/en/kuchnia/lunch-menu)**\n\n![](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1723111100_17061063329244-115a93092.jpg)\n\n### Unique Italian Lunches\n\nPRICE: 39 PLN per person\n\nOur lunches are available for you:  \nMonday – Friday: 13:00 – 16:00\n\nA 10% service charge is added to each order\n\nTel: +48 517 882 593  \nE-mail: fb.manager@crownkrakow.pl\n\n[CHECK MENU](https://www.crownhotel.pl/en/kuchnia/lunch-menu)\n\n* ![Caffè Bona](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/banners/1723110645_16939968167225-3.jpg)\n\n  # Caffè Bona\n\n![Caffè Bona](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1696597122_16938330283877-projektbeznazwy9.jpg)\n\n## Caffè Bona\n\n## Italian Desserts\n\nIndulge in a touch of dolce far niente – the sweet art of doing nothing – accompanied by perfectly crafted desserts. Our menu features classic pastries that are essential in any Italian restaurant. You will always find authentic cannoli and tiramisu here. But that’s not all – each day we offer our guests special desserts of the day. Every sweet treat is presented with artistic precision, designed to delight not only the taste buds but also the eyes.\n\n![Caffè Bona](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1696597185_16939037585131-prawdziwybaraperitivoplejadawoskichalkoholiicoktailipobudzamyapetytitworzymyniezapomnianatmosfer2.jpg)\n\n## Caffè Bona\n\n## Exceptional Coffee\n\nWe know – just like the Italians – that serving coffee is an art in itself. Our skilled baristas understand this perfectly. They have mastered the preparation of both classic espresso and creative coffee compositions. At Bona Restaurant, coffee is roasted just before serving, ensuring the freshest and richest flavor. We select beans from renowned plantations around the world to deliver our guests a perfectly balanced taste every time.\n\n* ![Bona Lounge Bar](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/banners/1723109351_16940754545889-krktcpgmae1.jpg)\n\n  # Bona Lounge Bar\n\n  A true aperitivo bar, offering a diverse selection of Italian spirits and cocktails. We awaken the appetite and create an unforgettable atmosphere.\n\n![BONA BAR](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1696597401_16940754551244-krktcpgmae28.jpg)\n\n## BONA BAR\n\n## Stylish Bar in Cracow\n\nOur bar is a space where elegance meets timeless sophistication. Inspired by the Italian aperitivo bar and the subtle ambiance of an English lobby bar, it creates an atmosphere of comfortable luxury. Carefully selected details, such as the marble flooring, give the interior a refined character. The cool texture of the stone perfectly contrasts with the warmth of the leather furnishings. Altogether, it forms a space that seems lifted straight from iconic Italian films.\n\n[CHECK MENU](https://profitroom-uploads.fra1.digitaloceanspaces.com/thecrownhotelhandwrittencollection/16946864420204_KRK_TC_KARTA%20NAPOI%20BISTRO%20BONA.pdf)\n\n![BONA BAR](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1696597461_16939032230631-2.jpg)\n\n## BONA BAR\n\n## Signature Cocktails\n\nOur cocktail menu is designed for the most discerning connoisseurs. Each signature drink is the result of our bartenders’ passion and expertise. The specialists craft unique flavor compositions, perfectly aligned with the character of the venue – rich, yet balanced.\n\n[CHECK MENU](https://profitroom-uploads.fra1.digitaloceanspaces.com/thecrownhotelhandwrittencollection/16946864162698_KRK_TC_KARTA%20BAR%20BONA.pdf)\n\n* ![Daily specials](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/banners/1758552677_dsc-0047.jpg)\n\n  # Daily specials\n\n  We offer daily promotions. Just like the Italians, we want to celebrate – and invite you to celebrate – every single day!\n\n![](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1758552944_dsc-0094.jpg)\n\n**Monday – Pasta e Basta!**\n\nStart the week with two pasta dishes for the price of one. It’s the perfect way to share Italian flavors with friends. At Bona Restaurant, every pasta is made from the highest quality ingredients, preserving the authentic taste of Italy. Something familiar… but reinvented!\n\n**Tuesday – Specialità del Martedì**\n\nTuesday is the time for three of our specialties at a 20% discount – indulge in discovering new flavors and experimenting with Italian cuisine to recharge after Monday!\n\n**Wednesday – Dolce Mercoledì**\n\nWednesday at Bona Restaurant is a true celebration of sweets: every dessert at 50% off. Savor Italian delicacies, from classic tiramisu to modern interpretations of ice creams and creams – the perfect midweek sweet treat.\n\n**Thursday – Amici & Piattini**\n\nThursday is all about gatherings in a relaxed, friendly atmosphere: order three small plates, and get a bottle of wine for just 59 PLN. At Bona Restaurant, we make sure every visit is a unique experience, filled with Italian warmth and hospitality.\n\n**Friday – Venerdì di Mare**\n\nFriday is dedicated to seafood lovers. Enjoy a complimentary glass of perfectly paired wine with every seafood dish. A refined way to start the weekend in elegant Italian style.\n\n**Saturday – La Cena Romantica**\n\nSaturday at Bona Restaurant means a candlelit dinner with live music. Enjoy a set of main course + dessert + wine at 20% off – all to celebrate special moments together in an exceptional atmosphere.\n\n**Sunday – Domenica in Famiglia**\n\nSunday is family time. Our family buffet is priced at 69 PLN for adults and 39 PLN for children up to 8 years old – the perfect opportunity to enjoy Italian flavors, vibrant presentations, and a relaxed, friendly atmosphere together.\n\n**Celebrate every day!**\n\n* ![](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/banners/1786966248_tarta-morelowo-malinowa-fs-3.jpeg)\n\n  FOR SEASON\n\n![](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1786966391_tatar-fs-3.jpeg)\n\nThe changing seasons inspire us to continuously explore new flavors and culinary techniques. From this passion for discovery, the culinary project \"For Season\" was born.\n\nJoin us and discover together what new delights seasonal cuisine has to offer! Every month, our team of chefs, drawing from the abundance of nature, carefully selects fresh, local ingredients to create dishes that highlight the very best of the season.\n\n## September / October\n\n**Main Course**  \nTagliatelle with roasted duck and plum ragù, horseradish crème fraîche,  \nand pickled celery  \n*69 PLN*\n\n**Cocktail**  \nGolden Plum  \nPlum-infused Jose Cuervo tequila, Tawny Port, grenadine, celery syrup, sour, egg white, orange bitters.  \n*39 PLN*\n\n**Menu prepared by:**  \nPiotr Schroeder, Chef of Porto 102 restaurant\n\n**Cocktail composed by:**  \nŁukasz Selwon, F&B Manager of Porto 102 restaurant\n\nThe FOR SEASON project was created in collaboration with restaurants belonging to the PI Apartamenty & Hotele Group: [Bona Restaurant](https://www.bonarestaurant.pl/en/), [Kuryer Restaurant](https://www.kuryerrestaurant.pl/en/), [Sobre de la Mesa](https://sobre-mesa-restaurant.bookero.pl/en), [Garnish Food&Wine](https://www.garnishrestaurant.pl/en/) and [Porto 102](https://porto102.bookero.pl/).\n\n* ![Tasting dinners](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/banners/1758275019_115a9241.JPG)\n\n  # Tasting dinners\n\n  Discover the magic of Italian cuisine during a special event that will transport you straight to sunny Italy.\n\n![](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1758274659_dsc-1624.jpg)\n\nWe invite you to our signature tasting dinners in the heart of Kraków. This unforgettable experience combines unique flavors, carefully selected wines, and captivating live music. Tasting dinners at Bona Restaurant delight not only with taste but also with their story…\n\nThe next editions of this event will take place soon - [check our website](https://www.crownhotel.pl/en) and stay updated!\n\n* ![Special Events](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/banners/1729065719_17071249664347-grafikidostroninewsletterw20240205t102135440.jpg)\n\n  # Special Events\n\nBona Restaurant is a place where the Italian atmosphere, elegant interiors and friendly service create a unique aura, perfect for celebrating important moments in life. Let us make your special occasion an unforgettable event, filled with taste, joy and a unique atmosphere.\n\n## What can we organise for you?\n\nWe invite you to take advantage of our professional service for the organisation of intimate receptions dedicated to groups of up to 40 people. Whatever the occasion, our offer includes comprehensive event organisation, taking care of every detail to create unforgettable moments for you and your loved ones.\n\n## Communions and Christenings\n\nIn our elegant interiors, we will create a unique atmosphere for communion celebrations and baptisms. Personalised menus will satisfy the most delicate palates, and carefully selected decorations will bring a unique character to this sacred event.\n\n## Birthdays and anniversaries\n\nWe offer a menu tailored to your favourite flavours. We will ensure that every element of the reception emphasises the individuality and uniqueness of the anniversary being celebrated.\n\n## Baby shower\n\nCelebrate the time of waiting for your little one with us! Themed decorations, healthy snacks and an atmosphere of joy will create a special atmosphere for mothers-to-be and their guests.\n\n## Bachelorette party\n\nFor brides-to-be, we will organise a reception that will highlight the energy of this special night. Enjoy and savour an exquisite menu and delicious wines that will add to the charm of the whole celebration.\n\n## Wake\n\nIn difficult times, we provide discreet organizational support. Our menu, reminiscent of tradition, will allow you to honor the memory of your loved ones in an intimate and respectful atmosphere.\n\n![](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1729066185_17071245219157-grafikidostroninewsletterw20240205t101405492.jpg)\n\n## Let's create a unique event together!\n\nPhone: + 48 517 882 593  \nE-mail: [fb.manager@crownkrakow.pl](mailto:fb.manager@crownkrakow.pl)\n\nTake a look at our sample menus for special events. We will, of course, be happy to adapt them to your expectations and are happy to meet any requirements.\n\n[CHCEK SAMPLE MENU](https://profitroom-uploads.fra1.digitaloceanspaces.com/thecrownhotelhandwrittencollection/17252687493156_KRK_TC_MENU%20OKOLICZNO%C5%9ACIOWE.pdf)\n\n* ![ZONE 508](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/banners/1723109941_16935782935951-pg2307171031.jpg)\n\n  # ZONE 508\n\n![ZONE 508](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1696597681_16935782935951-pg2307171031.jpg)\n\n### An intimate room on the 5th floor, a dining area for 6 people, a chillout zone and a private rooftop terrace. A personalised offer, dedicated to up to 5 people looking for individual business areas combined with catering and private service.\n\n### The minimum rental cost of Zone 508 is £7,000 net to be used for consumption and alcohol, in case of a lower order the difference of the amount will be supplemented by the missing value to be charged.\n\n### \n\n## You are welcome to book:\n\n### Tel.+48 517 882 589\n\n### E-mail: [gm@crownhotel.com](mailto:gm@crownhotel.com)\n\n### Reservations are accepted 24 hours in advance.\n\n[MORE](https://www.crownhotel.pl/en/spotkania/konferencje/strefa-508)\n\n![](https://cdn.bookero.pl/data/pages/7883/textimage/1756726996_dsc-0224.jpg)\n\n## CONTACT\n\nul. Królewska 6, 30-045 Kraków\n\nPhone: +48 12 629 90 03\n\nEmail: [fb.manager@crownkrakow.pl](mailto:fb.manager@crownkrakow.pl)\n\n**GODZINY OTWARCIA**\n\nMonday - Sunday: 01:00 pm - 10:00 pm\n\nMonday - Friday: 06:30 am - 01:00 pm\n\nSaturday - Sunday: 07:00 am - 01:00 pm.\n\nOur website uses cookies. While I think that that crunch is the best part, if you don't eat meat you should mention that you don't want this type of condiment and that you'll be happy with melted butter alone, otherwise your \"vegetarian\" choice will come not so very vegetarian.\n\n## Traditional Polish cuisine in a folkloristic setting ($$)",

      "raw_content": "[Skip to content](#main)\n\n\n[![Electric Blue Food - Kitchen stories from abroad](https://electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/EBF_logo_400_2.jpg)](https://electricbluefood.com/)\n\n[![Electric Blue Food - Kitchen stories from abroad](https://electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/EBF_logo_400_2.jpg)](https://electricbluefood.com/)\n\n\n\n# Where to eat Polish food in Krakow\n\nby[Eva](https://electricbluefood.com)  Originally posted:   Updated: \n\n[9 Comments](#comments)\n\nSo you find yourself in Krakow, the charming second largest city in Poland, and you wonder what it has to offer in terms of food. Krakow is very international and you can really find it all. From sushi to Mexican, from Italian to Ukrainian, Hungarian or even an American bakery, Krakow has it all. And it does not disappoint either when it comes to the local cuisine. You can find many options in town where you can sample authentic Polish food in Krakow, and in this post I am presenting an overview of some such eateries. These are all trusted places where I used to eat when I was an expat in Krakow, and love to recommend.\n\nKrakow has been skyrocketing in popularity as a tourism destination over the past decade. Krakow is a city with an exciting international vibe and numerous exchange students and expats call it home. I have lived in Krakow myself for over 2 years, and visited countless of times because of my family ties there. While I am one of the lucky ones who used to get real Polish food made by a real Polish grandma, even visitors that don't have a *Babcia* ready to fill their plates will have many opportunities to eat Polish food in Krakow.\n\nAs a former expat there I have thoroughly enjoyed Krakow's foodie scene and amazing restaurants and cafeterias. I have already written about [my personal favourite eateries in town](//electricbluefood.com/2016/12/30/a-foodies-guide-to-krakow/) as well as an overview of its [captivating hipster foodie scene](//electricbluefood.com/2017/07/14/a-hipster-taste-of-krakow/). This time I am covering the restaurants that offer Polish cuisine, so where to eat Polish food in Krakow.\n\n![Explore with me the restaurants where you can sample authentic Polish food in Krakow. These are all places I recommend because I used to eat there when I was an expat in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/where-to-eat-3.png)\n\n## Polish food basics: where to get cheap pierogi ($)\n\nLet's start from the cheaper options. There are two eateries that I recommend when you want to try Polish food on a budget. While dining prices in Poland are comparatively lower than those of Western Europe, you may still want to keep the food budget as low as possible. In that case you should head to [U Babci Maliny](http://www.kuchniaubabcimaliny.pl/) (ul. Szpitalna 38; or ul. Sławkowska 17) or [Gospoda Koko](http://www.gospodakoko.pl/) (ul. Gołębia 8). Both restaurants are small, informal and cheap. U Babci Maliny has an extensive menu offer, while Gospoda Koko's forte is the deal of the day, where you can have a full meal for as little as 15 PLN (about 3,5€).\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-02.jpg) \n\nU Babci Maliny on ul. Sławkowska 17\n\nWhile their menu offer does not only include **pierogi**, that's what I always end up eating because they're such a classic. (And cheap, did I already say that?). Pierogi are the most famous item of the Polish cuisine, also thanks to Polish immigrants, who have been produly exporting pierogi all over the world. If pierogi have never crossed your path, what you need to know is that they're a type of dumplings. The filling can feature various ingredients including cheese and potatoes (*pierogi ruskie*), cabbage and mushrooms (*pierogi z kapustą i grzybami**)*, meat (*pierogi z mięsem*), or even blueberries ([*pierogi z jagodami*](//electricbluefood.com/2015/08/18/something-polish-pierogi-z-jagodami/)). The great thing about pierogi is that they are very easy to find as nearly every restaurant has them on offer.\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-01.jpg) \n\nPierogi ruskie with a glass of *kompot*\n\nWhile it can be hard to fully enjoy traditional Polish cuisine as a vegetarian, there are a few meatless options and *pierogi ruskie* is one of these. The filling is made with cheese and potatoes, and they're generally served boiled, with a topping of melted butter. A common addition is *skwarki*, or small dices of fried pork rind. While I think that that crunch is the best part, if you don't eat meat you should mention that you don't want this type of condiment and that you'll be happy with melted butter alone, otherwise your \"vegetarian\" choice will come not so very vegetarian.\n\n## Traditional Polish cuisine in a folkloristic setting ($$)\n\nIf you have a medium budget and want to experience Polish eating in a fun environment and with a bit of a folkloristic feel, I cannot recommend enough the restaurants of the \"Kompania Kuflowa\" group: [Pod Wawelem](http://podwawelem.eu/) (ul. Świętej Gertrudy 26-29) and [Restauracja Sukiennice](http://sukiennice-restauracja.pl/en/) (Rynek Główny 3). Don't let the location of either drive you into thinking that these restaurants are tourist traps. While well-equipped to cater for tourists, both have an excellent reputation also among locals. And both locations are just great. I mean, enjoying a beer right in the heart of the most beautiful square in Poland? Yes, please! (Call me biased, Krakow's Rynek Główny is the prettiest square in Poland to me.)\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-03.jpg) \n\nBeer (and pickles) with a view!\n\nAs much as I need to recommend these restaurants, I also need to recommend that you go there for the meat! Traditional Polish cuisine is very meat-oriented and if you eat meat this is an aspect of this food culture you may really appreciate. Particularly if you like pork, these restaurants will keep you very happy. Their **Wiener schnitzel** is epic (so large!), and so is the roasted **pork knuckle**, traditionally served with mustard and freshly grated horseradish. Accompany these full flavours with a pint or three of delicious **Polish beer** and you'll leave really happy.\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-04.jpg) \n\nThis is a large schnitzel!\n\nAnother restaurant that has a lovely folkloristic feel and offers excellent Polish food in a very reasonable price range is [Morskie Oko](http://www.morskieoko.krakow.pl/home-en/) (Plac Szczepański, 8). Named after a lake in the Tatra Mountains, the restaurant's décor is inspired by the folklore of the highlands. Carved wooden forniture, tablecloths in the traditional mountain floral patterns, and the hearty food offer will make you feel like you are somewhere around Zakopane (a lovely mountain town that makes for a great day-trip from Krakow). This is the perfect place to sample the traditional mountain-style grilled **smoked cheese with cranberry jam** (*ser „Gazdowski\" z żurawiną*) or one of the traditional soups. Their pierogi are really notable too, the meat ones probably being my favourite in all of the city!\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-05.jpg) \n\nProbably the best *pierogi z mięsem*in town\n\n ![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-06.jpg) \n\nMountain-style smoked cheese, crispy from the grill.\n\n## Feast like a king from three centuries ago ($$$)\n\nIf budget is not an issue and you feel like splurging on food during your Krakow trip, then you have to head to [Ogniem i Mieczem](http://www.ogniemimieczem.pl/index_en.html) (pl. Serkowskiego 7). This restaurant prides itself offering traditional cuisine from the 17th century, a time when the kindgom of Poland reached out as far as present-day Lithuania and Ukraine. If you've been spending your Krakow holiday learning about the Kings that ruled these lands a few centuries ago, now it is time to step back in time and feast like one.\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-07.jpg) \n\nOgniem i Mieczem - details of the interior\n\nExpect to find pierogi, potatoes, various cuts of meat - all of the finest possible quality. Among my favourite dishes there is the duck breast with garlic sauce, or the **half metre of pork ribs**. While not a big fan of ribs, Ogniem i Mieczem takes the credits for being the restaurant that serves my favourite. The last time I visited they were recommending their cooked ham. We were a party of 4 and shared a portion of ham and a half metre of ribs. We also indulged on baked potatoes and a large serving of mixed pierogi of various fillings. The act of sharing the food with your tablemates had quite a medieval feeling indeed.\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-08.jpg) \n\nAn ode to pork, that's what it is.\n\n ![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-09.jpg) \n\nA generous serve of mixed pierogi. Each filling has a different shape.\n\nThis restaurant is a bit over the average price range of restaurants in town, but the food is really excellent and the ambience, including the location of the restaurant in the charming district of Podgórze, make the experience really wirth it if you are looking for a more upscale place to eat during your stay in Krakow.\n\n## Polish street food you have to try\n\nIf you prefer to grab something to eat on the go rather than sit in a restaurant, Krakow's got you covered. Among the street food in town there are two food items that are truly important in Krakow. One is the ***obwarzanek krakowski*** (which some, including me, call *precel*). It is a ring-shaped bread that is quite similar to a bagel, but with a larger diameter and larger hole.\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-12.jpg)\n\nTraditionally *obwarzanek* comes in three variants: with salt, poppy seeds or sesame seeds (here listed by personal preference, salt is my number 1! But for the photo I chose poppy seeds because I'm incoherent). This bread snack is deeply rooted in the city of Krakow, where it has the status of regional food with protected geographical indication. You can buy those from street carts found all around the city. The price is really low, averaging at 2 PLN (0,5€) and the vendors only take cash. It is my favourite thing to munch on when I'm roaming the city centre and I want to have something to eat in between meals. [Bunkier Café](https://en.bunkiercafe.pl/) (pl. Szczepański 3a) is one of the first places in town that started serving obwarzanek in a sit-down way: toasted and filled. I strongly recommend the vegetarian one, filled with spinach, baba ghanoush, mozzarella and salsa.\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-13.jpg) \n\nFilled obwarzanek from Bunkier Café.\n\nThe other signature food that you can buy from stalls and eat out in the street is ***zapiekanka***. Zapiekanka is a toasted open sandwich made on baguette bread. The basic version features button mushrooms and cheese, with a generous splash of ketchup on top. Just like pizza, you can find many other toppings to choose from if you fancy something with more flavour. This is my [zapiekanka recipe](https://electricbluefood.com/zapiekanka-polish-toasted-sandwich/).\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-15.jpg) \n\nClassic zapiekanka (middle) and flavour versions on the sides.\n\nThe place to go to for your zapiekanka fix is undoubtedly Plac Nowy in the Jewish quarter Kazimierz. There are several stalls to choose from, and the general rule of thumb is that the one with the longest queue usually has the best zapiekanka. So if you are not on the run you can choose according to this rule. I have eaten from stalls with a shorter line (I'm a rebel) and was never disappointed. In the end there is not much wrong you can do with bread, mushrooms and cheese.\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-14.jpg) \n\nPlac Nowy in Kazimierz, home of the real zapiekanka.\n\nZapiekanka is ranked among the best street foods you can find in Europe, and I very much agree. If you want to know more, here's the compete list of [great street foods in Europe](https://thewingedfork.com/50-best-street-food-in-europe/).\n\n## Polish bakeries and the Pope's favourite dessert\n\nWhile Poland might not be very famous for its desserts, there are two items of the Polish confectionery tradition that I absolutely must recommend. Do not forget that Krakow has been part of the Austro-Hungarian Empire, the same that introduced [Sachertorte](//electricbluefood.com/2016/08/19/sachertorte-my-viennese-challenge/) to the world, as well as the coffee culture in Europe (more about it [here](//electricbluefood.com/2018/04/27/trieste-food-coffee-culture/)). So my first recommendation for you is ***pączek z różą***.\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-11.jpg)\n\n*Pączek z różą* is a doughnut (krapfen) filled with rose petal jam (here's my [recipe for rose petal preserve](//electricbluefood.com/2017/08/11/rose-petals-in-sugar/)) and a light sugar glaze on top. The one pictured here also features bits of candied orange peel on top. Traditionally eaten on Fat Thursday all over Poland, you can find them year round in most bakeries. On the street where my family lives there is a shop from the bakery chain [Buczek](http://pieczywo-buczek.pl/) that sells my favourite doughnuts with rose filling. Being a chain, Buczek bakeries are found all over the city, including a pretty big one on the lowest level of the shopping mall Galeria Krakowska.\n\nEven if you think you want to pass on the rose-flavoured pastry, do yourself a favour and visit a **Polish bakery** anyway. You'll find something else to satisfy your cravings for sure, plus most bakeries offer breakfast deals with a bread roll and coffee for very little money. Back to desserts, my sweet recommendation number two is also something you can find at a Buczek bakery, or you can head to a pastry shop to get it. It's called ***kremówka**.*\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](//electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-krakow-10.jpg) \n\nThe Polish Pope's favourite dessert, as declared by John Paul II himself.\n\n*Kremówka* is a cream pie made of two layers of puff pastry filled with a layer of vanilla custard cream (the basic version) as well as a layer of whipped cream (the best version, humbly according to me). The pastry shop where I tried my first *kremówka* at age 7 was [Cukiernia Czarodziej](http://www.czarodziej.krakow.pl/) on ul. Karmelicka 15 (all locations [here](http://www.czarodziej.krakow.pl/kontakt/)). Although I really like the one from Buczek - as I already said it's so close to my grandparents' house it's the fastest way to fix my cravings - I guess nothing tastes better than the first one, right? So go visit the sorcerer (that's what *czarodziej* means in Polish) and try this divine food. Hey, if even a Pope claimed it to be his favourite dessert, it truly must have a godly quality!\n\nI have recently learned that this kind of cream pie is quite popular all over Eastern Europe; and I was raised to believe this was a Polish-only thing. Other countries have their own *kremówka* too, like I learned when reading an article on Slovenia's *kremna rezina* (in Italian, [here](http://www.berightback.it/2018/04/kremna-rezina/)). I actually promised the author of that article I would \"soon\" write about the Polish version of this dessert, so here I am! Next time my mom visits, maybe we could even try to make it at home, then the recipe will also follow. Doesn't this sound *sweet*?\n\nThis post does not feature any soup at all. I am not a big fan of soups, especially when I visit in the summer. Loyal to my habits, I did not order any soup in any of the restaurants I visited. Got offered it at home, meh. If soups are your thing, or you visit at a time of the year when a hot bowl of soup is actually a very good idea, you'll find a soup section in the menu of every Polish restaurant. I hope this post has inspired you to visit Krakow, a city that has a restaurant for all tastes, including some fine places where to sample real Polish cuisine.\n\nPin my Polish food recommendations for your future foodie trip to Krakow.\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](https://electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/polish-food-3.png)\n\n![Where to eat Polish food in Krakow. A guide to my favourite restaurants that offer Polish cuisine in Krakow.](https://electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/07/Polish-food-6.png)\n\n### Polish recipes you have to try at home\n\n* [Pomidorowa, a flavourful tomato soup](//electricbluefood.com/zupa-pomidorowa-polish-tomato-soup/) whitened with sour cream.\n\n## More countries\n\n* [![](https://electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/12/nz-06.jpg) \n\n  Campervanning New Zealand in winter](https://electricbluefood.com/campervanning-new-zealand-winter/)\n* [![cliffs of moher on a rainy day](https://electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/05/western-ireland-cliffs-moher-4.jpg) \n\n  Our Ireland itinerary: 7 days between Ireland and Northern Ireland](https://electricbluefood.com/ireland-itinerary-7-days/)\n* [![Streets of Vilnius, Lithuania](https://electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2019/03/vilnius-lithuania-03.jpg) \n\n  Two days in Vilnius: what to see and what to eat](https://electricbluefood.com/two-days-in-vilnius/)\n* [![Visit Istanbul on a layover](https://electricbluefood.com/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/istanbul-layover-dinner-01.jpg) \n\n  Rooftop dining in Istanbul on a layover](https://electricbluefood.com/rooftop-dining-istanbul-layover/)\n\n## 9 Comments\n\n1. I haven't visited Krakow for a while and I'm sure it's changed. The first time I visited there were very few restaurants and lots of the old style canteens. Glad to know that traditional Polish food is still available in this beautiful city.\n\n   [Reply](#comment-3101)\n\n   1. I am guessing the canteens you refer to are the \"milk bars\", the super cheap eateries from the Communism era. Yeah now it's very much changed, the city has put on a total new look over the past 15 years. But traditional food has not been lost, I guess more and more people are taking it as a cultural asset worth exploring.\n\n      [Reply](#comment-3102)\n2. My maternal grandmother was Ukrainian and my father's side of the family Polish so we grew up eating the best pierogi ...these ones look amazing. I am long overdue for a trip to Poland so you've given me an extra boost of inspiration. I'm pinning for later\n\n   [Reply](#comment-3104)\n\n   1. Oh how wonderful you got to taste the real stuff in your family! When you make it to Poland you're gonna love the food, many restaurants succeeded at keeping traditional cooking alive so it's really easy to find authentic food.\n\n      [Reply](#comment-3105)\n3. I love exploring local vegetarian food and non-alcoholic drinks wherever I travel to, so I absolutely love this post. 🙂 I've never had a chance to try out Polish food, but it does look very interesting. Thank you for this detailed post.\n\n   [Reply](#comment-3106)\n\n   1. Poland is most famous for the meat and beer, but you can definitely feast there too even if you want to skip the meat and the alcohol. It's just a little more challenging, but totally possible.\n\n      [Reply](#comment-3107)\n4. Finalmente riesco a leggere questo tuo interessantissimo post. Oltre a dei locali veramente particolari, mi hai fatto conoscere tantissimi cibi polacchi che proprio non conoscevo e che mi fanno tantissima gola. Tra l'altro scatti delle foto spettacolari. Ora non ho proprio più scuse per visitare Cracovia <3\n\n   [Reply](#comment-3288)\n\n   1. Grazie cara! Siccome i tuoi commenti si approvano da soli perché su questo blog sono i benvenuti, questo tuo ultimo intervento mi era completamente sfuggito. Me ne scuso!! Volevo ringraziarti per aver preso del tempo per leggere queste mie raccomandazioni. Eh sì, devi assolutamente andare a Cracovia e assaggiare tutte queste prelibatezze!
