For more information, click on district number.\n\n## Economy\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=14 \"Edit section: Economy\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dc/Biurowiec_Newton.JPG/250px-Biurowiec_Newton.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Biurowiec_Newton.JPG)\n\nThe Centre for Business Innovation office complex in Kraków\n\nKraków is one of Poland's most important economic centres and the economic hub of the [Lesser Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lesser_Poland \"Lesser Poland\") (Małopolska) region.[[170]](#cite_note-gosp-172)[[171]](#cite_note-krakplgosp-173) Since the fall of communism, the private sector has been growing steadily. There are about 50 large [multinational companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multinational_companies \"Multinational companies\") in the city, including [Google](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google \"Google\"), [Uber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uber \"Uber\"), [IBM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IBM \"IBM\"), [Shell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shell_plc \"Shell plc\"), [UBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UBS \"UBS\"), [HSBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HSBC \"HSBC\"), [Motorola](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Motorola \"Motorola\"), [Aptiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aptiv \"Aptiv\"), [MAN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MAN_SE \"MAN SE\"), [General Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric \"General Electric\"), [ABB](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ABB \"ABB\"), [Aon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aon_(company) \"Aon (company)\"), [Akamai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Akamai \"Akamai\"), [Cisco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cisco \"Cisco\"), [Hitachi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hitachi \"Hitachi\"), [Altria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Altria \"Altria\"), [Capgemini](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capgemini \"Capgemini\"),[[172]](#cite_note-capgemini-174) and [Sabre Holdings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sabre_Holdings \"Sabre Holdings\"),[[173]](#cite_note-About_Sabre_Holdings-175) along with other British, German and Scandinavian-based firms.[[170]](#cite_note-gosp-172)[[174]](#cite_note-pbs-176) The city is also the global headquarters for [Comarch](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Comarch \"Comarch\"), an enterprise software house. Kraków is the second most-visited city in Poland (after Warsaw).[[170]](#cite_note-gosp-172)[[171]](#cite_note-krakplgosp-173) According to the World Investment Report 2011 by the UN Conference for Trade and Development (UNCTAD), Kraków is also the most emergent city location for investment in global BPO projects (Business Process Outsourcing) in the world.[[175]](#cite_note-PAIiIZ-177)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/Unity_Tower%2C_front%2C_2_Lubomirskiego_street%2C_Krakow%2C_Poland.jpg/250px-Unity_Tower%2C_front%2C_2_Lubomirskiego_street%2C_Krakow%2C_Poland.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Unity_Tower,_front,_2_Lubomirskiego_street,_Krakow,_Poland.jpg)\n\n[Unity Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unity_Tower \"Unity Tower\") is one of the tallest buildings in the city.\n\nIn 2011, the [city budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budget_of_Kraków \"Budget of Kraków\"), which is presented by the [Mayor of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Kraków \"Mayor of Kraków\") on 15 November annually, has a projected revenue of 3,500,000,000 [złoty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Złoty \"Złoty\").[[176]](#cite_note-krakow2-178) The primary sources of revenue were as follows: 14% from the municipal taxation on real estate properties and the use of [amenities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amenities \"Amenities\"), 30% in transfers from the national [budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budget \"Budget\"), and 34% in state subsidies. Projected expenditures, totalling 3.52 billion złoty, included 21% in city development costs and 79% in city maintenance costs. Of the maintenance costs, as much as 39% were spent on education and childcare. The City of Kraków's development costs included; 41% toward construction of roads, transport, and communication (combined), and 25% for the city's infrastructure and environment.[[177]](#cite_note-Biuletyn_Statystyczny_Miasta_Krakowa-179) The city has a high [bond credit rating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bond_credit_rating \"Bond credit rating\"), and some 60% of the population is under the age of 45.[[171]](#cite_note-krakplgosp-173)\n\n[Unity Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unity_Tower \"Unity Tower\") was completed in 2020 after almost 30 years, creating a new business and residential centre. It is the second-tallest building in the city after [K1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K1_(building) \"K1 (building)\").[[178]](#cite_note-180)\n\n### Knowledge and innovation community\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=15 \"Edit section: Knowledge and innovation community\")]\n\nKraków is one of the co-location centres of Knowledge and Innovation Community (Sustainable Energy) of The [European Institute of Innovation and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Institute_of_Innovation_and_Technology \"European Institute of Innovation and Technology\") (EIT).[[179]](#cite_note-Knowledge_and_Innovation_Community_EIT,_at-181)\n\nInnoEnergy is an integrated alliance of reputable organisations from the education, research and industry sectors. It was created based on long standing links of cooperation as well as the principles of excellence. The partners have jointly developed a strategy to tackle the weaknesses of the European innovation landscape in the field of sustainable energy.[[180]](#cite_note-europa-182)\n\n## Transport\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=16 \"Edit section: Transport\")]\n\nMain article: [Transport in Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Kraków \"Transport in Kraków\")\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/00/Krakow%2C_tram_NGT6-2_n%C2%B02039.JPG/250px-Krakow%2C_tram_NGT6-2_n%C2%B02039.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Krakow,_tram_NGT6-2_n°2039.JPG)\n\n[Bombardier city tram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bombardier_Transportation \"Bombardier Transportation\") on Piłsudski Bridge\n\nPublic transport is based on a fairly dense network of tram and bus routes operated by a municipal company, supplemented by a number of private minibus operators. There is no [rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit \"Rapid transit\") system in Kraków, but a [metro line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_Metro \"Kraków Metro\") is planned. First works are expected to commence in 2028.[[181]](#cite_note-183)[[182]](#cite_note-184) Local trains connect some of the suburbs. The bulk of the city's historic area has been turned into a [pedestrian zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedestrian_zone \"Pedestrian zone\") with rickshaws and horse-drawn carriages; however, the trams run within a three-block radius.[[183]](#cite_note-Krakow._Varied_Means_of_Transportation-185) The historic means of transportation in the city can be examined at the [Museum of Municipal Engineering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muzeum_Inżynierii_Miejskiej_w_Krakowie \"Muzeum Inżynierii Miejskiej w Krakowie\") in the Kazimierz district, with many old trams, cars and buses.[[184]](#cite_note-186)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/33/20150207_Pendolino_ED250_PKP_Intercity_Krakow_5031.jpg/250px-20150207_Pendolino_ED250_PKP_Intercity_Krakow_5031.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:20150207_Pendolino_ED250_PKP_Intercity_Krakow_5031.jpg)\n\n[PKP Intercity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PKP_Intercity \"PKP Intercity\") train at the [Main Railway Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_Główny \"Kraków Główny\")\n\nRailway connections are available to most [Polish cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Poland \"List of cities and towns in Poland\"), e.g. [Katowice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katowice \"Katowice\"), [Częstochowa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Częstochowa \"Częstochowa\"), [Szczecin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Szczecin \"Szczecin\"), [Gdynia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gdynia \"Gdynia\") and [Warsaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw \"Warsaw\"). International destinations include [Bratislava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bratislava \"Bratislava\"), [Budapest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Budapest \"Budapest\"), [Vienna](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vienna \"Vienna\"), [Prague](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prague \"Prague\"), [Berlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berlin \"Berlin\"), [Hamburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hamburg \"Hamburg\"), [Lviv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lviv \"Lviv\"), [Kyiv](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv \"Kyiv\"), and [Odesa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Odesa \"Odesa\") (June–September).[[185]](#cite_note-International_railway_connections_from_Kraków-187) The [main railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_Główny_railway_station \"Kraków Główny railway station\") is located just outside the Old Town District and is well-served by public transport.[[186]](#cite_note-188)\n\nKraków's airport, officially named [Kraków John Paul II International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_John_Paul_II_International_Airport \"Kraków John Paul II International Airport\") ([IATA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/IATA_airport_code \"IATA airport code\"): **KRK**), is located 11 km (7 mi) west of the city. Direct trains cover the route between [Kraków Główny train station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_Główny \"Kraków Główny\") and the airport in 20 minutes. Kraków Airport served around 5,800,000 passengers in 2017.[[187]](#cite_note-189) Also, the [Katowice International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katowice_International_Airport \"Katowice International Airport\") is located 80 kilometres (50 miles) or about 75 minutes from Kraków.[[188]](#cite_note-e-krakow-190)\n\nIn Autumn 2016 Poland's oldest [Bicycle-sharing system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicycle-sharing_system \"Bicycle-sharing system\") was modernized and now offers 1,500 bikes at 169 stations under the name of Wavelo *[(pl)](https://pl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wavelo \"pl:Wavelo\")*, which is owned by BikeU of the French multinational company Egis.[[189]](#cite_note-191)\n\n## Demographics\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=17 \"Edit section: Demographics\")]\n\nSee also: [Urban demographics of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Poland#Largest_metropolitan_and_urban_areas \"Demographics of Poland\")\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Krak%C3%B3w_population_pyramid.svg/250px-Krak%C3%B3w_population_pyramid.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kraków_population_pyramid.svg)\n\nKraków population pyramid in 2021\n\nKraków had a recorded population of 774,839 in 2019, which increased to 804,237 in 2023.[[190]](#cite_note-demografia.stat.gov.pl-192) Selected demographic indicators are presented in a table (below), compiled on the basis of only the population living in Kraków permanently. The larger [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_metropolitan_area \"Kraków metropolitan area\") of the city encompasses a territory in which (in 2010) 1,393,893 inhabitants live.[[191]](#cite_note-stat-193)\n\nAlready in the Middle Ages, the population of Kraków consisting of numerous ethnic groups, began to grow rapidly.[[192]](#cite_note-Kras-194) It doubled between 1100 and 1300 from 5,000 to 10,000, and in 1400 counted 14,000 inhabitants. By 1550, the population of metropolitan Kraków was 18,000; although it decreased to 15,000 in the next fifty years due to calamity.[[193]](#cite_note-Keene-195)[[194]](#cite_note-Sedlar-196) By the early 17th century the Kraków population had reached 28,000 inhabitants.[[195]](#cite_note-Labno-197)\n\nIn the historical 1931 census preceding World War II, 78.1% of Cracovians declared Polish as their primary language, with Yiddish or Hebrew at 20.9%, Ukrainian 0.4%, German 0.3%, and Russian 0.1%.[[196]](#cite_note-encyklopedia-198) The ravages of history have greatly reduced the percentage of [ethnic minorities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_minorities \"Ethnic minorities\") living in Kraków.\n\nIn the last two decades, Kraków has seen a large growth of immigrant population. In the 2002 census, only 0.25% of respondents living in the city declared a non-Polish nationality primarily.[[197]](#cite_note-199) As of 2019, it was estimated that foreigners accounted for as much as 10% of the city's population.[[198]](#cite_note-200)\n\nForeign residents (2023)[[199]](#cite_note-201)\n\n| Nationality | Population |\n| [Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine \"Ukraine\") | 45,100 |\n| [Belarus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belarus \"Belarus\") | 5,975 |\n| [Georgia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georgia_(country) \"Georgia (country)\") | 3,640 |\n| [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India \"India\") | 2,636 |\n| [Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia \"Russia\") | 2,221 |\n| [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italy \"Italy\") | 1,512 |\n\nPopulation growth in Kraków since 1791\n\n![](//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/timeline/22dj43ipx9oybbufiss1zxw34pficz6.png)\n\n### Religion\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=18 \"Edit section: Religion\")]\n\nMain articles: [Churches of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Churches_of_Kraków \"Churches of Kraków\") and [Synagogues of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synagogues_of_Kraków \"Synagogues of Kraków\")\n\nReligion in Krakow (2021)[[200]](#cite_note-202)\n\n1. [Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholicism \"Catholicism\") (58.0%)\n2. [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism \"Protestantism\") (0.50%)\n3. Other Christian (0.20%)\n4. Other (0.01%)\n5. [Irreligion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Not_religious \"Not religious\") (14.0%)\n6. Undeclared (27.0%)\n\nThe metropolitan city of Kraków has more than 300 churches[[201]](#cite_note-203) and is known as the [city of churches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_of_Churches \"City of Churches\"). The abundance of historic landmark temples along with the plenitude of monasteries and convents earned the city a countrywide reputation as the \"Northern Rome\" in the past. The churches of Kraków comprise over 120 places of worship (2007) of which over 65 were built in the 20th century. More are still being added.[[202]](#cite_note-diecezja-204) In addition to [Roman Catholicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholicism \"Roman Catholicism\"), other denominations present include [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah's_Witnesses \"Jehovah's Witnesses\"),[[203]](#cite_note-205) [Mariavite Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariavite_Church \"Mariavite Church\"), Polish Catholic Church, [Polish Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Orthodox_Church \"Polish Orthodox Church\"), [Protestantism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism \"Protestantism\") and [Latter-Day Saints](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latter-Day_Saints \"Latter-Day Saints\").[[204]](#cite_note-206) As of 2017, weekly Mass attendance in the [Archdiocese of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Kraków \"Archdiocese of Kraków\") was 49.9 percent, above the national Polish average of 38.3 percent.[[205]](#cite_note-207)\n\nKraków contains also an outstanding collection of monuments of Jewish [sacred architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacred_architecture \"Sacred architecture\") unmatched anywhere in Poland. Kraków was an influential centre of Jewish spiritual life before the outbreak of World War II, with all its manifestations of religious observance from [Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthodox_Judaism \"Orthodox Judaism\") to [Hasidic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hasidic_Judaism \"Hasidic Judaism\") and [Reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_Judaism \"Reform Judaism\") flourishing side by side. There were at least 90 synagogues in Kraków active before the [Nazi German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazi_German \"Nazi German\") invasion of Poland, serving its burgeoning Jewish community of 60,000–80,000 (out of the city's total population of 237,000), established since the early 12th century.[[206]](#cite_note-Dylewski-208)\n\nMost synagogues of Kraków were ruined during World War II by the [Nazis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazis \"Nazis\") who despoiled them of all ceremonial objects, and used them as storehouses for ammunition, firefighting equipment, as general storage facilities and stables. The post-[Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holocaust \"Holocaust\") Jewish population of the city had dwindled to about 5,900 before the end of the 1940s. Poland was the only [Eastern Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Bloc \"Eastern Bloc\") country to allow free Jewish [aliyah](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aliyah \"Aliyah\") (emigration to Israel) without visas or exit permits upon the conclusion of World War II.[[207]](#cite_note-D-H-209) In recent time, thanks to efforts of the local Jewish and Polish organisations including foreign financial aid from the [American Jewish Joint Distribution Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Jewish_Joint_Distribution_Committee \"American Jewish Joint Distribution Committee\"), many synagogues underwent major restorations and serve religious and tourist purposes.[[208]](#cite_note-jewish-guide-210)\n\n## Education\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=19 \"Edit section: Education\")]\n\nMain article: [Education in Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Kraków \"Education in Kraków\")\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bb/UEK_Budynek_G%C5%82%C3%B3wny_%28od_frontu%29.jpg/250px-UEK_Budynek_G%C5%82%C3%B3wny_%28od_frontu%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:UEK_Budynek_Główny_(od_frontu).jpg)\n\n[Kraków University of Economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_University_of_Economics \"Kraków University of Economics\")\n\nKraków is a major centre of education. Twenty-four institutions of higher education offer courses in the city, with more than 200,000 students.[[209]](#cite_note-211) [Jagiellonian University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_University \"Jagiellonian University\"), the oldest university in Poland and ranked by the *[Times Higher Education Supplement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Times_Higher_Education_Supplement \"Times Higher Education Supplement\")* as the second-best university in the country,[[210]](#cite_note-THES-212)[[211]](#cite_note-Jagiellonian_University_ranking_among_world_universities-213) was founded in 1364 as *Studium Generale*[[212]](#cite_note-214) and renamed in 1817 to commemorate the royal [Jagiellonian dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_dynasty \"Jagiellonian dynasty\") of Poland and Lithuania.[[213]](#cite_note-jagiellonian-215) Its principal academic asset is the [Jagiellonian Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_Library \"Jagiellonian Library\"), with more than 4 million volumes, including a large collection of medieval manuscripts[[214]](#cite_note-Treasures_of_the_Jagiellonian_Library-216) like [Copernicus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copernicus \"Copernicus\")' *[De Revolutionibus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/De_Revolutionibus \"De Revolutionibus\")* and the [Balthasar Behem Codex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balthasar_Behem_Codex \"Balthasar Behem Codex\"). With 42,325 students (2005) and 3,605 academic staff, the Jagiellonian University is also one of the leading research centres in Poland. Famous historical figures connected with the university include [Saint John Cantius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Cantius \"John Cantius\"), [Jan Długosz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Długosz \"Jan Długosz\"), [Nicolaus Copernicus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolaus_Copernicus \"Nicolaus Copernicus\"), [Andrzej Frycz Modrzewski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrzej_Frycz_Modrzewski \"Andrzej Frycz Modrzewski\"), [Jan Kochanowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Kochanowski \"Jan Kochanowski\"), [King John III Sobieski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_John_III_Sobieski \"King John III Sobieski\"), [Pope John Paul II](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pope_John_Paul_II \"Pope John Paul II\") and Nobel laureates [Ivo Andrić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivo_Andrić \"Ivo Andrić\") and [Wisława Szymborska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisława_Szymborska \"Wisława Szymborska\").[[215]](#cite_note-Wislawa_Szymborska,_Nobel_Prize_in_Literature_1996-217)\n\nAGH University of Science and Technology, established in 1919, is the largest [technical university](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_technology \"Institute of technology\") in Poland, with more than 15 faculties and student enrollment exceeding 30,000.[[216]](#cite_note-AGH-UST_staff_and_students,_introduction-218) It was ranked by the Polish edition of *[Newsweek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newsweek \"Newsweek\")* as the best technical university in the country in 2004.[[217]](#cite_note-agh-219) During its 80-year history, more than 73,000 students graduated from AGH with master's or bachelor's degrees. Some 3,600 persons were granted the degree of [Doctor of Science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctor_of_Science \"Doctor of Science\"), and about 900 obtained the qualification of *[Doctor habilitatus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doctor_habilitatus \"Doctor habilitatus\")*.[[218]](#cite_note-History_of_AGH-UST-220)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Collegium_Maius_2017.jpg/330px-Collegium_Maius_2017.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Collegium_Maius_2017.jpg)\n\n*[Collegium Maius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collegium_Maius \"Collegium Maius\")*, [Jagiellonian University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_University \"Jagiellonian University\")'s oldest building\n\nOther institutions of higher learning include [Academy of Music in Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Music_in_Kraków \"Academy of Music in Kraków\") first conceived as [conservatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_school \"Music school\") in 1888, one of the oldest and most prestigious conservatories in Central Europe and a major concert venue;[[219]](#cite_note-History_of_the_Akademia_Muzyczna-221) [Kraków University of Economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_University_of_Economics \"Kraków University of Economics\"), established in 1925;[[220]](#cite_note-Kraków_University_of_Economics_homepage-222) [Pedagogical University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pedagogical_University_of_Cracow \"Pedagogical University of Cracow\"), in operation since 1946;[[221]](#cite_note-Akademia_Pedagogiczna_w_Krakowie,_homepage-223) [Agricultural University of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agricultural_University_of_Kraków \"Agricultural University of Kraków\"), offering courses since 1890 (initially as a part of [Jagiellonian University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_University \"Jagiellonian University\"));[[222]](#cite_note-Akademia_Rolnicza,_homepage-224) [Academy of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Matejko_Academy_of_Fine_Arts \"Jan Matejko Academy of Fine Arts\"), the oldest [Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fine_Arts \"Fine Arts\") Academy in Poland, founded by the [Polish painter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_painter \"Polish painter\") Jan Matejko; [Ludwik Solski Academy for the Dramatic Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludwik_Solski_Academy_for_the_Dramatic_Arts \"Ludwik Solski Academy for the Dramatic Arts\");[[223]](#cite_note-krakow4-225) [The Pontifical Academy of Theology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pontifical_Academy_of_Theology \"The Pontifical Academy of Theology\");[[224]](#cite_note-Papieska_Akademia_Teologiczna,_homepage_in_English-226) [AGH University of Science and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AGH_University_of_Science_and_Technology \"AGH University of Science and Technology\") and [Kraków University of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tadeusz_Kościuszko_University_of_Technology \"Tadeusz Kościuszko University of Technology\"), which has more than 37,000 graduates.\n\nScientific societies and their branches in Kraków conduct scientific and educational work in local and countrywide scale. The [Academy of Learning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Learning \"Academy of Learning\"), [Association of Law Students' Library of the Jagiellonian University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_of_Law_Students'_Library_of_the_Jagiellonian_University \"Association of Law Students' Library of the Jagiellonian University\"), [Polish Copernicus Society of Naturalists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Copernicus_Society_of_Naturalists \"Polish Copernicus Society of Naturalists\") and the Polish Section of [Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Institute_of_Electrical_and_Electronics_Engineers \"Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers\") all have their main seats in Kraków.\n\n## Culture\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=20 \"Edit section: Culture\")]\n\nMain articles: [Culture of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Kraków \"Culture of Kraków\"), [Events in Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Events_in_Kraków \"Events in Kraków\"), and [Kraków Old Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_Old_Town \"Kraków Old Town\")\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Lady_with_an_Ermine_-_Leonardo_da_Vinci_%28adjusted_levels%29.jpg/250px-Lady_with_an_Ermine_-_Leonardo_da_Vinci_%28adjusted_levels%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lady_with_an_Ermine_-_Leonardo_da_Vinci_(adjusted_levels).jpg)\n\n[Leonardo da Vinci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_da_Vinci \"Leonardo da Vinci\")'s *[Lady with an Ermine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_with_an_Ermine \"Lady with an Ermine\")*, at the [Czartoryski Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czartoryski_Museum \"Czartoryski Museum\")\n\nKraków was named the official [European Capital of Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Capital_of_Culture \"European Capital of Culture\") for the year 2000 by the [European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Union \"European Union\").[[225]](#cite_note-European_Capital_of_Culture_in_Poland_again-227) Major landmarks include the [Main Market Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_Market_Square,_Kraków \"Main Market Square, Kraków\") with [St. Mary's Basilica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Mary's_Basilica,_Kraków \"St. Mary's Basilica, Kraków\") and the [Sukiennice Cloth Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukiennice \"Sukiennice\"), the [Wawel Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wawel_Castle \"Wawel Castle\"), the [National Art Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum,_Kraków \"National Museum, Kraków\"), the [Sigismund Bell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zygmunt_(bell) \"Zygmunt (bell)\") at the [Wawel Cathedral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wawel_Cathedral \"Wawel Cathedral\"), and the medieval [St. Florian's Gate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Florian's_Gate \"St. Florian's Gate\") with the [Barbican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_barbican \"Kraków barbican\") along the [Royal Coronation Route](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Road,_Kraków \"Royal Road, Kraków\").[[226]](#cite_note-krakow-info-228) Among them is the [Czartoryski Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czartoryski_Museum \"Czartoryski Museum\") featuring works by [Leonardo da Vinci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_da_Vinci \"Leonardo da Vinci\"), [Holbein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hans_Holbein_the_Younger \"Hans Holbein the Younger\") and [Rembrandt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rembrandt \"Rembrandt\") as well as the [Archaeological Museum of Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archaeological_Museum_of_Kraków \"Archaeological Museum of Kraków\") whose collection highlights include the [Zbruch Idol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zbruch_Idol \"Zbruch Idol\") and the [Bronocice Pot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronocice_pot \"Bronocice pot\").[[227]](#cite_note-229)\n\nThe [Lajkonik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lajkonik \"Lajkonik\"), a bearded man dressed as a [khan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Khan_(title) \"Khan (title)\") on a [hobby horse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hobby_horse \"Hobby horse\"), is a prominent element of intangible cultural heritage associated with Kraków.[[228]](#cite_note-lajkonik-230) The tradition dates back to [folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore \"Folklore\") surrounding the [13th-century Mongol invasion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Mongol_invasion_of_Poland \"First Mongol invasion of Poland\"), when the local [timber raftsmen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timber_rafting \"Timber rafting\") successfully repelled a siege on the village of [Zwierzyniec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zwierzyniec,_Kraków \"Zwierzyniec, Kraków\").[[228]](#cite_note-lajkonik-230) The raftsmen subsequently entered Kraków wearing the captured attire of the Mongol commander as a victory demonstration.[[228]](#cite_note-lajkonik-230) The ritual procession accompanied by musicians takes place annually eight days after the [Feast of Corpus Christi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feast_of_Corpus_Christi \"Feast of Corpus Christi\").[[228]](#cite_note-lajkonik-230) The oldest written records of the performance date to the mid-18th century and the ritual has been maintained continuously as an expression of civic identity.[[228]](#cite_note-lajkonik-230)\n\nA trumpet call (*[hejnał mariacki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hejnał_mariacki \"Hejnał mariacki\")*) is sounded every hour from St. Mary's Basilica in the Market Square.[[229]](#cite_note-231) The melody, which used to announce the opening and closing of city gates, ends unexpectedly in midstream. According to legend, the tune was played during the Mongol invasion by a guard warning citizens against the attack. Whilst playing, he was shot by an archer of the invading forces and the bugle call broke off at the moment he died.[[230]](#cite_note-hejnal-232) The story is recounted in [Eric P. Kelly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eric_P._Kelly \"Eric P. Kelly\")'s 1928 book *[The Trumpeter of Krakow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Trumpeter_of_Krakow \"The Trumpeter of Krakow\")*, which won a [Newbery Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Newbery_Award \"Newbery Award\").[[231]](#cite_note-JPKelly-233)\n\nThe city also maintains a rich culinary history, with [obwarzanek krakowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obwarzanek_krakowski \"Obwarzanek krakowski\") and the [bagel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bagel \"Bagel\") originating in Kraków.[[232]](#cite_note-234) Obwarzanek, a braided, ring-shaped bread product, holds a distinct status in European culinary heritage, protected under the European Union's [Protected Geographical Indication (PGI)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographical_indications_and_traditional_specialities_in_the_European_Union \"Geographical indications and traditional specialities in the European Union\") framework.[[233]](#cite_note-235)\n\n### Museums and national art galleries\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=21 \"Edit section: Museums and national art galleries\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Entrance_Lobby_of_the_National_Museum_in_Krak%C3%B3w%2C_2022.jpg/250px-Entrance_Lobby_of_the_National_Museum_in_Krak%C3%B3w%2C_2022.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Entrance_Lobby_of_the_National_Museum_in_Kraków,_2022.jpg)\n\nThe [National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum,_Kraków \"National Museum, Kraków\") in Kraków is one of Poland's finest galleries of art.\n\nAs of 2023[[update]](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit), Kraków hosts approximately 82 museums and various museum branches; the city also has a number of art collections and public art galleries.[[234]](#cite_note-Heritage_Team-236) The [National Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Museum_in_Kraków \"National Museum in Kraków\"), established in 1879, as well as the National Art Collection on Wawel Hill, are all accessible to the general public.[[234]](#cite_note-Heritage_Team-236)\n\nThe Royal Chambers at Wawel feature art, period furniture, Polish and European paintings, collectibles, and a major collection of 16th-century monumental [Flemish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flemish_people \"Flemish people\") [tapestries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tapestries \"Tapestries\").[[234]](#cite_note-Heritage_Team-236) Wawel Treasury and Armoury features Polish royal memorabilia, jewels, applied art, and 15th- to 18th-century arms.[[234]](#cite_note-Heritage_Team-236) The Wawel Eastern Collection features [Turkish tents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ottoman_tents \"Ottoman tents\") and military accessories.[[234]](#cite_note-Heritage_Team-236) The National Museum holds the largest body of artworks in the country with collections consisting of several hundred thousand items kept mostly in the Main Building at 3 Maja Street, although there are eleven other separate divisions of the museum in the city, one of the most popular being the [Gallery of 19th Century Polish Art at Sukiennice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sukiennice_Museum \"Sukiennice Museum\") which houses a collection of some of the best-known paintings and sculptures of the [Young Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Poland \"Young Poland\") movement.[[234]](#cite_note-Heritage_Team-236) Inaugurated in 2013, the latest division of the National Museum is the *[Europeum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EUROPEUM_–_European_Culture_Centre \"EUROPEUM – European Culture Centre\")*, with works by [Brueghel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pieter_Brueghel_the_Younger \"Pieter Brueghel the Younger\") among a hundred Western European paintings.[[235]](#cite_note-mkidn.gov.pl-237)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ef/Krakow_-_Congress_Centre_from_balloon_-_1.jpg/250px-Krakow_-_Congress_Centre_from_balloon_-_1.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Krakow_-_Congress_Centre_from_balloon_-_1.jpg)\n\nKraków Congress Centre, the city's business and cultural flagship\n\nOther notable museums in Kraków include the [Manggha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manggha \"Manggha\") Museum of Japanese Art and Technology (at M. Konopnickiej 26),[[236]](#cite_note-Wyspiański-238) [Stanisław Wyspiański](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanisław_Wyspiański \"Stanisław Wyspiański\") Museum (at 11 Szczepanska St),[[236]](#cite_note-Wyspiański-238) [Jan Matejko Manor House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Matejko_Manor_House \"Jan Matejko Manor House\") in Krzesławice,[[68]](#cite_note-Matejko-70) the [Emeryk Hutten-Czapski Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emeryk_Hutten-Czapski_Museum \"Emeryk Hutten-Czapski Museum\"), devoted to the master painter and his life,[[237]](#cite_note-Czapski-239) and [Józef Mehoffer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Józef_Mehoffer \"Józef Mehoffer\") Manor.[[236]](#cite_note-Wyspiański-238)\n\nThe [Rynek Underground](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rynek_Underground \"Rynek Underground\") museum, situated under the city's main square, showcases Kraków's more than 1,000-year history through its streets, activities and artifacts. The construction of the museum was preceded by extensive excavations starting in 2005,[[238]](#cite_note-240) and continuing eventually until 2010, as more and more physical evidence was uncovered.\n\nThe [Polish Aviation Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Aviation_Museum \"Polish Aviation Museum\"), considered one of the world's best aviation museums by [CNN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CNN \"CNN\"),[[239]](#cite_note-241) features over 200 aircraft including a [Sopwith Camel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sopwith_Camel \"Sopwith Camel\") among other First World War biplanes, a comprehensive display of aero engines, and a complete collection of airplane types developed by Poland after 1945.[[240]](#cite_note-242) Activities of smaller museums around Kraków and in the Lesser Poland region are promoted and supported by the [Małopolska Institute of Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Małopolska_Institute_of_Culture \"Małopolska Institute of Culture\"), which organises annual Małopolska Heritage Days.[[241]](#cite_note-243)\n\n### Performing arts\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=22 \"Edit section: Performing arts\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e5/Juliusz_S%C5%82owacki_Theatre%2C_Krak%C3%B3w%2C_Poland%2C_September_2019.jpg/250px-Juliusz_S%C5%82owacki_Theatre%2C_Krak%C3%B3w%2C_Poland%2C_September_2019.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Juliusz_Słowacki_Theatre,_Kraków,_Poland,_September_2019.jpg)\n\nKraków's [Juliusz Słowacki Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juliusz_Słowacki_Theatre \"Juliusz Słowacki Theatre\")\n\nThe city has several famous theatres, including the Narodowy Stary Teatr (the [National Old Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Old_Theatre \"National Old Theatre\")),[[242]](#cite_note-Stary_Teatr_w_Krakowie,_homepage_in_Polish-244) the [Juliusz Słowacki Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juliusz_Słowacki_Theatre \"Juliusz Słowacki Theatre\"), the Bagatela Theatre, the [Ludowy Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ludowy_Theatre \"Ludowy Theatre\"), and the Groteska Theatre of Puppetry, as well as the [Opera Krakowska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opera_Krakowska \"Opera Krakowska\") and Kraków Operetta. The city's principal concert hall and the home of the [Kraków Philharmonic Orchestra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_Philharmonic_Orchestra \"Kraków Philharmonic Orchestra\") is the [Kraków Philharmonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_Philharmonic \"Kraków Philharmonic\") (*Filharmonia Krakowska*) built in 1931.[[243]](#cite_note-KP-245)\n\nKraków hosts many annual and biannual artistic events,[[244]](#cite_note-krakow.pl-1-246) some of international significance such as the Misteria Paschalia (Baroque music), Sacrum-Profanum (contemporary music), the Kraków Screen Festival ([popular music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Popular_music \"Popular music\")), the Festival of [Polish Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Poland \"Music of Poland\") (classical music), Dedications (theatre), the [Kraków Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_Film_Festival \"Kraków Film Festival\") (one of Europe's oldest short films events),[[245]](#cite_note-Krakowski_Festiwal_Filmowy-247) [Etiuda&Anima International Film Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etiuda&Anima_International_Film_Festival \"Etiuda&Anima International Film Festival\") (the oldest international art-film event in Poland), Biennial of [Graphic Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Graphic_Arts \"Graphic Arts\"), and the [Jewish Culture Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jewish_Culture_Festival \"Jewish Culture Festival\"). Kraków was the residence of two Polish [Nobel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Prize \"Nobel Prize\") laureates in literature, [Wisława Szymborska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisława_Szymborska \"Wisława Szymborska\") and [Czesław Miłosz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czesław_Miłosz \"Czesław Miłosz\"); a third [Nobel laureate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Nobel_laureates \"List of Nobel laureates\"), the Yugoslav writer [Ivo Andrić](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivo_Andrić \"Ivo Andrić\"), lived and studied in Kraków. Other former longtime residents include internationally renowned [Polish film](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_film \"Polish film\") directors [Andrzej Wajda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrzej_Wajda \"Andrzej Wajda\") and [Roman Polanski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Polanski \"Roman Polanski\"), both of whom are [Academy Award](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_Award \"Academy Award\") winners.[[246]](#cite_note-Ain-Krupa-248)\n\n### Music\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=23 \"Edit section: Music\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/04/Krak%C3%B3w_-_Filharmonia_01.jpg/250px-Krak%C3%B3w_-_Filharmonia_01.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Kraków_-_Filharmonia_01.jpg)\n\nConcert hall of the [Kraków Philharmonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_Philharmonic \"Kraków Philharmonic\")\n\n[Opera Krakowska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opera_Krakowska \"Opera Krakowska\")[[247]](#cite_note-krakowska-249) one of the leading national opera companies, stages 200 performances each year including ballet, operettas and musicals. It has, in its main repertoire, the greatest world and Polish opera classics. The Opera moved into its first permanent House in the autumn of 2008. It is in charge also of the *Summer Festival of Opera and Operetta*.\n\nKraków is home to two major Polish festivals of [early music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_music \"Early music\") presenting forgotten Baroque oratorios and operas: *Opera Rara*,[[248]](#cite_note-Opera_Rara_–_Kraków_–_8_December_2011-250) and *Misteria Paschalia*.[[249]](#cite_note-misteriapaschalia-251) Meanwhile, [Capella Cracoviensis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capella_Cracoviensis \"Capella Cracoviensis\") runs the *Music in Old Krakow International Festival*.[[250]](#cite_note-252)\n\nAcademy of Music in Kraków, founded in 1888, is known worldwide as the *alma mater* of the contemporary Polish composer [Krzysztof Penderecki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krzysztof_Penderecki \"Krzysztof Penderecki\") and it is also the only one in Poland to have two winners of the [International Chopin Competition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Chopin_Competition \"International Chopin Competition\") in Warsaw among its alumni. The academy organises concerts of its students and guests throughout the whole year.[[251]](#cite_note-History_–_Krakow_Music_Academy-253)\n\nMusic organisations and venues include: Kraków Philharmonic,[[252]](#cite_note-krakow_fil-254) *Sinfonietta Cracovia* (a.k.a. the Orchestra of the Royal City of Kraków), the Polish Radio Choir of Kraków, *Organum* Academic Choir, the Mixed [Mariański Choir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariański_Choir \"Mariański Choir\") (*Mieszany Chór Mariański*), Kraków Academic Choir of the [Jagiellonian University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_University \"Jagiellonian University\"), the Kraków Chamber Choir, *Amar Corde* String Quartet, *Consortium Iagellonicum* Baroque Orchestra of the [Jagiellonian University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_University \"Jagiellonian University\"), Brass Band of T. Sendzimir Steelworks, and *Camerata* Chamber Orchestra of Radio Kraków.\n\n## Tourism\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=24 \"Edit section: Tourism\")]\n\nAccording to official statistics, in 2019 Kraków was visited by over 14 million tourists including 3.3 million foreign travellers.[[253]](#cite_note-Graczyński-255) The visitors spent over 7.5 billion zloty (ca. €1.7 billion) in the city (without travel costs and pre-booked accommodation). Most foreign tourists came from Germany (14.2%), United Kingdom (13.9%), Italy (11.5%), France (11.2%), Spain (10.4%) and Ukraine (5.4%).[[253]](#cite_note-Graczyński-255) The Kraków tour-guide from the Lesser Poland Visitors Bureau indicated that not all statistics are recorded due to the considerable number of those who come, staying in readily available private rooms paid for by cash, especially from [Eastern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_Europe \"Eastern Europe\").[[254]](#cite_note-rp.pl/398262-256)\n\nThe main reasons for visiting the city are: its historical monuments, recreation as well as relatives and friends (placing third in the ranking), religion and business. There are 120 quality hotels in Kraków (usually about half full) offering 15,485 overnight accommodations.[[255]](#cite_note-intur.com-257) The average stay lasts for about 4 to 7 nights. The survey conducted among the travelers showed that they enjoyed the city's friendliness most, with 90% of Polish tourists and 87% foreigners stating that they would recommend visiting it.[[254]](#cite_note-rp.pl/398262-256) Notable points of interest outside the city include the [Wieliczka Salt Mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wieliczka_Salt_Mine \"Wieliczka Salt Mine\"), the [Tatra Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatra_Mountains \"Tatra Mountains\") 100 km (62 mi) to the south, the historic city of [Częstochowa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Częstochowa \"Częstochowa\") (north-west), the well-preserved former Nazi concentration camp at [Auschwitz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auschwitz-Birkenau_State_Museum \"Auschwitz-Birkenau State Museum\"), and [Ojcowski National Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ojcowski_National_Park \"Ojcowski National Park\"),[[256]](#cite_note-Krakow_Day_Trips-258) which includes the Renaissance Castle at [Pieskowa Skała](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pieskowa_Skała \"Pieskowa Skała\").[[257]](#cite_note-Top_10_things_to_do_in_Kraków-259) Kraków has been awarded a number of top international rankings such as the 1st place in the *Top city-break destinations 2014* survey conducted by the British consumer association *[Which?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Which%3F \"Which?\")*.[[258]](#cite_note-260)\n\n## Sports\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=25 \"Edit section: Sports\")]\n\n[Football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Association_football \"Association football\") is the most popular sport in the city.[[259]](#cite_note-Krakow_Sport_Information_Guide-261) The two [football teams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_team \"Football team\") with the largest following are thirteen-time Polish champion [Wisła Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wisła_Kraków \"Wisła Kraków\"),[[260]](#cite_note-Wisła-262) and five-time champion [Cracovia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cracovia_(football_club) \"Cracovia (football club)\"),[[261]](#cite_note-Czarni_Pogoń-263) both founded in 1906 as the oldest still existing in Poland.[[262]](#cite_note-skwk.pl-264) They have been involved in the most intense rivalry in the country and one of the most intense in all of Europe, known as the [Holy War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_War_(Kraków) \"Holy War (Kraków)\") (**Święta Wojna**).[[263]](#cite_note-footballderbies.com-265) Other football clubs include [Hutnik Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hutnik_Kraków \"Hutnik Kraków\"), [Wawel Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wawel_Kraków \"Wawel Kraków\"), [Wieczysta Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wieczysta_Kraków \"Wieczysta Kraków\") and one-time Polish champion [Garbarnia Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Garbarnia_Kraków \"Garbarnia Kraków\"). There is also the first-league rugby club [Juvenia Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juvenia_Kraków \"Juvenia Kraków\"). Kraków has a number of additional, equally valued sports teams including twelve-time Polish [ice hockey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ice_hockey \"Ice hockey\") champions [Cracovia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KS_Cracovia_(ice_hockey) \"KS Cracovia (ice hockey)\") and the twenty-time women's basketball champions Wisła Kraków.[*[citation needed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed \"Wikipedia:Citation needed\")*] The [Cracovia Marathon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cracovia_Marathon \"Cracovia Marathon\"), with thousands of participants from two dozen countries annually, has been held in the city since 2002.[[264]](#cite_note-Marathon-266)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d4/KrakowArenaWidokOdNowejHuty_%28cropped%29.jpg/250px-KrakowArenaWidokOdNowejHuty_%28cropped%29.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:KrakowArenaWidokOdNowejHuty_(cropped).jpg)\n\n[Tauron Arena Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tauron_Arena_Kraków \"Tauron Arena Kraków\")\n\nThe construction of a new [Tauron Arena Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tauron_Arena_Kraków \"Tauron Arena Kraków\") began in May 2010; for concerts, indoor athletics, hockey, basketball, [futsal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Futsal \"Futsal\") and other events. The facility has an area of 61,434 m2, with a maximum arena court area of 4,546 m2. The average capacity is 18,000 for concerts, and 15,000 for sport events, with the maximum number of spectators being 22,000.[[265]](#cite_note-about_us-267) The Arena boasts Poland's largest [LED](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LED \"LED\") media façade, with a total surface of 5,200 m2 of LED strip lighting, wrapping around the stadium, and one of Europe's largest LED screens, measuring over 540 m2.[[266]](#cite_note-268)\n\nKraków was the host city of the [2014 FIVB Men's Volleyball World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIVB_Men's_Volleyball_World_Championship \"2014 FIVB Men's Volleyball World Championship\") and [2016 European Men's Handball Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_European_Men's_Handball_Championship \"2016 European Men's Handball Championship\"). It was also selected as the European City of Sport for 2014.[[267]](#cite_note-aktualnosci_2014-269) Kraków was [bidding](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kraków_bid_for_the_2022_Winter_Olympics \"Kraków bid for the 2022 Winter Olympics\") to host the [2022 Winter Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Winter_Olympics \"2022 Winter Olympics\") with [Jasná](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jasná \"Jasná\") but the bid was rejected by a majority (69.72%) of the vote in [a referendum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Kraków_referendum \"2014 Kraków referendum\") on 16 May 2014.[[268]](#cite_note-270) Krakow and the Malopolska region hosted the [2023 European Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_European_Games \"2023 European Games\") from 21 June to 2 July 2023. More than 7,000 athletes representing 49 countries participated.[[269]](#cite_note-271)\n\n## Notable people\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=26 \"Edit section: Notable people\")]\n\nFurther information: [List of people from Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_people_from_Kraków \"List of people from Kraków\")\n\n## International relations\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=27 \"Edit section: International relations\")]\n\nSee also: [List of twin towns and sister cities in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_twin_towns_and_sister_cities_in_Poland \"List of twin towns and sister cities in Poland\")\n\n### Consulates\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=28 \"Edit section: Consulates\")]\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/77/Tenement-Consulate_General_of_the_United_States%2C_9_Stolarska_street%2C_Old_Town%2C_Krakow%2C_Poland.jpg/120px-Tenement-Consulate_General_of_the_United_States%2C_9_Stolarska_street%2C_Old_Town%2C_Krakow%2C_Poland.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Tenement-Consulate_General_of_the_United_States,_9_Stolarska_street,_Old_Town,_Krakow,_Poland.jpg)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/Old_brewery%27s_administration_building%2C_17h_Lubicz_Street%2C_Krak%C3%B3w%2C_Poland.JPG/250px-Old_brewery%27s_administration_building%2C_17h_Lubicz_Street%2C_Krak%C3%B3w%2C_Poland.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Old_brewery's_administration_building,_17h_Lubicz_Street,_Kraków,_Poland.JPG)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/70/Maria_Lewalska_villa%2C_42_Krupnicza_Street%2C_Krak%C3%B3w%2C_Poland.jpg/250px-Maria_Lewalska_villa%2C_42_Krupnicza_Street%2C_Krak%C3%B3w%2C_Poland.jpg?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Maria_Lewalska_villa,_42_Krupnicza_Street,_Kraków,_Poland.jpg)\n\n[![](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/09/A-282_kamienica_Krak%C3%B3w_ul._Stolarska_15_MM.JPG/120px-A-282_kamienica_Krak%C3%B3w_ul._Stolarska_15_MM.JPG?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:A-282_kamienica_Kraków_ul._Stolarska_15_MM.JPG)\n\nFrom top, left to right: consulates general of the United States, Hungary, Austria and France\n\nThere are eight consulates general in Kraków – [Austria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austria \"Austria\"), [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France \"France\"), [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Germany \"Germany\"), [Hungary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hungary \"Hungary\"), [Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia \"Russia\"), [Slovakia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slovakia \"Slovakia\"), [Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine \"Ukraine\"), [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States \"United States\"), three honorary consulates general – [India](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/India \"India\"), [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japan \"Japan\"), [Turkey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turkey \"Turkey\"), 24 honorary consulates – [Belgium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belgium \"Belgium\"), [Bulgaria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bulgaria \"Bulgaria\"), [Chile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chile \"Chile\"), [Colombia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colombia \"Colombia\"), [Croatia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Croatia \"Croatia\"), [Denmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denmark \"Denmark\"), [Estonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Estonia \"Estonia\"), [Finland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Finland \"Finland\"), [Iceland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iceland \"Iceland\"), [Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia \"Indonesia\"), [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Italy \"Italy\"), [Latvia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latvia \"Latvia\"), [Lithuania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lithuania \"Lithuania\"), [Luxembourg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luxembourg \"Luxembourg\"), [Mexico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico \"Mexico\"), [Mongolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongolia \"Mongolia\"), [Netherlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Netherlands \"Netherlands\"), [Norway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Norway \"Norway\"), [Pakistan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pakistan \"Pakistan\"), [Peru](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peru \"Peru\"), [Romania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romania \"Romania\"), [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spain \"Spain\"), [Sweden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sweden \"Sweden\"), [Uruguay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uruguay \"Uruguay\"), and a Representative of the [Government of Kurdistan Region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Kurdistan_Region \"Government of Kurdistan Region\").[[270]](#cite_note-272)\n\n### Twin towns and sister cities\n\n[[edit](/w/index.php?title=Krak%C3%B3w&action=edit&section=29 \"Edit section: Twin towns and sister cities\")]\n\nKraków is [twinned](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sister_city \"Sister city\"), or maintains close relations, with 36 cities around the world:[[271]](#cite_note-Kraków_partnerships-273)[[272]](#cite_note-Kraków_twins-274)[[273]](#cite_note-Kraków_honorary_twins-275)\n\n* [![Indonesia](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Flag_of_Indonesia.svg/40px-Flag_of_Indonesia.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia \"Indonesia\") [Batu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Batu,_East_Java \"Batu, East Java\"), [Indonesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesia \"Indonesia\") (2000)[[272]](#cite_note-Kraków_twins-274)\n* [![France](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/c/c3/Flag_of_France.svg/40px-Flag_of_France.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France \"France\") [Bordeaux](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bordeaux \"Bordeaux\"), France (1993)[[271]](#cite_note-Kraków_partnerships-273)\n* [![Slovakia](//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Flag_of_Slovakia.svg/40px-Flag_of_Slovakia.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&utm_campaign=parser&utm_content=thumbnail)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Slovakia \"Slovakia\") [Bratislava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bratislava \"Bratislava\"), Slovakia[[271]](#cite_note-Kraków_partnerships-273)[[274]](#cite_note-Bratislawa-276)\n* Also spelled in English as **Cracow**, or without Polish diacritics as **Krakow**.[[8]](#cite_note-8) [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin_language \"Latin language\"): *Cracovia*; [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_language \"German language\"): *Krakau*, pronounced [[ˈkʁaːkaʊ]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help:IPA/Standard_German \"Help:IPA/Standard German\") [ⓘ](/wiki/File:De-Krakau.ogg \"File:De-Krakau.ogg\").\n2. The park, which was initiated by Jordan, was the first of this type in Poland and in Europe. The initiator himself got so engaged in realising his idea that he largely financed its construction and personally brought the most modern sport facilities from Sweden, Germany and Switzerland.\n148.
